Ford Otosan at a

Management Discussion 
and Analysis Sustainability Corporate Statements General Assembly
Financial Statements

About the Report 
We are proud to present the financial, environmental, 
social, and governance performance of Ford Otomotiv 
Sanayi A.Ş. (Ford Otosan) for the period from January 1, 2025 
to December 31, 2025. The Annual Report 2025 covers our 
operations in Türkiye and Romania and includes the Board 
of Directors’ Report.
The Annual Report 2025 provides a comprehensive 
overview of our approach to global and industry trends, 
our Future. Now sustainability strategy, and the double 
materiality assessment. It highlights the material topics 
identified through this assessment, our performance 
and targets, our value creation model, and our corporate, 
strategic, risk, and opportunity management frameworks. 
Additionally, it outlines our stakeholder engagement, best 
practices, and our financial and operational performance in 
2025, along with our guidance for 2026.
We continue to shape all our sustainability-related 
efforts around our fair, transparent, and accountable 
governance approach. As a signatory to and member of 
the UN Global Compact, we integrate sustainability into 
all our business processes, aiming to contribute to the 
Sustainable Development Goals. We remain committed 
to complying with the UN Guiding Principles on Business 
and Human Rights, the International Labor Organization 
(ILO) Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights 
at Work, and the Organization for Economic Co-operation 
and Development (OECD) Guidelines for Multinational 
Enterprises.
As a company listed in the Borsa Istanbul (BIST) 
Sustainability Index, FTSE4Good Emerging Markets, and 
DIAL Global Index, we prioritize transparent disclosure of 
our sustainability performance on international platforms.
We actively participate in the CDP’s climate change and 
water security programs. Furthermore, conducted by 
S&P Global has earned us a place in The Sustainability 
Yearbook 2026. Furthermore, we have been included in S&P 
Global’s The Sustainability Yearbook 2026 as a result of our 
performance in the Corporate Sustainability Assessment 
(CSA).
In line with our emission reduction targets approved by 
the Science-Based Targets initiative (SBTi) in 2025, we 
diligently work toward our 2050 net-zero commitment. 
We integrate climate-related risks and opportunities into 
our strategic planning and risk management processes. By 
regularly updating our climate and water scenario analyses, 
we continue to reinforce our physical and transition risk 
assessments.
Compliance and Contents 
At Ford Otosan, we aim to provide all stakeholders with 
a clear understanding of our value creation efforts from 
short-, medium-, and long-term perspectives through 
our Annual Report. It meets the minimum requirements 
of the Capital Markets Law (CML) for annual reports. The 
Sustainability Principles Compliance Report is provided on 
page 90.
Our financial statements for 2024 and 2025 have been 
prepared in accordance with Turkish Financial Reporting 
Standards (TFRS) and the IAS 29 Financial Reporting in 
Hyperinflationary Economies standard, following the Public 
Oversight Authority’s (POA) Implementation Guide on 
Financial Reporting in Hyperinflationary Economies.
Audit
The financial data presented herein was audited by Güney 
Bağımsız Denetim ve Serbest Muhasebeci Mali Müşavirlik 
A.Ş. (EY), resulting in an assurance statement. The 
assurance statement for the financial data is provided on 
page 125 of this report.
The sustainability-related data and disclosures in this 
report have not yet been prepared according to the Turkish 
Sustainability Reporting Standards (TSRS) under the Public 
Oversight, Accounting, and Auditing Standards Authority 
(POA) regulations. They have also not been subject to the 
assurance audit required by the POA. A separate report, 
prepared in compliance with POA regulations to include 
the necessary data and disclosures under TSRS 1 and TSRS 
2, will be publicly disclosed within the statutory timeframe 
after an independent auditor conducts the required 
sustainability assurance audit.
For your questions, opinions and 
suggestions about this report: 
ir@ford.com.tr

Shareholding Structure and Investor Relations 
KOÇ HOLDİNG A.Ş. FORD MOTOR COMPANY*
Founded in 1926, Koç Holding has leading positions with 
strong competitive advantages in energy, automotive, 
consumer durables, and finance sectors. Today, Koç Holding 
is Türkiye’s leading investment holding company in terms 
of combined revenues, exports, number of employees, taxes 
paid, and total market capitalization on Borsa Istanbul. As 
Türkiye’s largest industrial and services group, Koç Group 
employs more than 120,000 people and operates in over 60 
countries. The Group’s revenues correspond to more than 7% 
of Türkiye’s GDP and exports.
Koç Holding ranks as the first Turkish company to enter the 
Fortune Global 500 list. Koç Group has been a driving force 
of the Turkish economy for a century and committed to a 
business model centered on sustainable value creation.
*Ford Deutschland Engineering GmbH: 100% owned by Ford Motor 
Company.
Ford Motor Company, a global company based in Dearborn, 
Michigan, is committed to freedom of movement that goes 
hand-in-hand with looking after the planet and each other. The 
company’s Ford+ plan for growth and value creation combines 
existing strengths, new capabilities and always-on relationships 
with customers to enrich experiences for customers and deepen 
their loyalty.
F Ford develops and delivers innovative, must-have Ford trucks, 
sport utility vehicles, commercial vans and vehicles and Lincoln 
luxury vehicles, along with connected services. The company 
does that through three customer-centered business segments:
• Ford Blue: Engineering iconic ICE and hybrid vehicles
• Ford Model e: Delivering breakthrough electric vehicles at 
scale, and developing native software and technologies to offer 
superior digital experiences for all customers.
• Ford Pro: Dedicated to delivering the most comprehensive 
vehicles and services to help commercial customers transform 
their businesses.
Additionally, Ford provides financial services through Ford Motor 
Credit Company. Ford employs about 170,000 people worldwide.

Relative Share Performance
Investor Relations Program in World Standards
Ford Otosan, a Group CDA share, is traded on BIST Stars. With its commitment 
to sustainability, Ford Otosan is also a constituent of BIST Sustainability Index, 
FTSE4Good Emerging Markets Index, Bloomberg Gender-Equality Index (as 
of 2021), and BIST Corporate Governance, which represent key indices for 
responsible investment decisions. Ford Otosan also responds to CDP’s climate 
change and water security programs and supports the Science-based Targets 
initiative (SBTi).
Ford Otosan, traded on the Borsa Istanbul Star Board, ranked as the most valuable 
automotive company and among the top ten overall among BIST companies in 2025. 
Borsa Istanbul rose by 24% on TL basis and BIST Industrial index by 5% in 2025, while Ford 
Otosan’s share price performed at a similar level year over year. Foreign share in free float 
stood at 34%.
Ford Otosan adopts an investor relations approach based on maintaining open, 
comprehensive, transparent, consistent and proactive communication with the investment 
community in compliance with regulatory requirements and corporate disclosure policies. 
The Investor Relations Team shares investor feedback in continuous contact with senior 
management and incorporates them into company strategies.
Ford Otosan Investor Relations Department has maintained continuous communication 
with the investment community through numerous activities and events in 2025.
The presentations and earnings releases for investors and analysts have been redesigned 
with clearer, modern, and more accessible content to increase engagement. The Investor 
Kit, containing our monthly presentations and production, sales, and export data, is 
uploaded to the corporate website every month. The Investor Relations Call Center has 
been launched to strengthen investor communications.

The Investor Relations Department attended in 23 roadshows and conferences and held 
numerous one-on-one meetings in 2025, meeting with a total of 660 institutional investors 
and analysts from Türkiye and abroad throughout the year.
Quarterly earnings calls were held with the CFO in attendance to provide information 
on the company’s financial performance during four online meetings in 2025 with 270 
investors and analysts. The department also shared these meetings’ audio recordings and 
transcripts along with company presentations on the corporate website for all investors to 
access.
During the reporting period, the company’s capital was increased from TL 350,910,000 to 
TL 3,509,100,000 TL, corresponding to a 900% bonus share issue and covered entirely by 
own assets. Furthermore, the company’s registered capital ceiling was increased from TL 
500 million to TL 10 billion and its expiration was extended until the end of 2029.
The investor relations department aims for Ford Otosan to reach its fair market value 
and share price and follows a dividend policy that ensures dividend distribution to the 
shareholders in alignment with long-term company strategy and financial discipline.
As the most valuable automotive company traded on Borsa Istanbul, Ford Otosan is 
followed by several Turkish and international intermediary institutions. As of year-end 
2025, Ford Otosan is on the list of 29 intermediary institutions, including 24 analysts 
recommending “Buy” and 3 recommending “Hold” for Ford Otosan stocks while 2 are 
“Currently Reviewing”. In 2025, the number of Turkish and international intermediary 
institutions publishing analyst reports on the company reached 79.
*Calculated according to the purchasing power as of 31 
December 2025.

main operations in the income statement are part of the company’s main operations, 
the adjusted EBITDA is calculated as the sum of Operating Profit (instead of EBIT), fixed 
expenses related to the leasing method (embedded lease effect) in accordance with IFRS 
16, and depreciation & amortization.

Key Financial Indicators
The Turkish automotive market delivered strong performance 
in 2025, increasing total sales volume by 10% to 1,408,135* 
units and once again breaking the previous year’s historical 
record. Passenger vehicle sales surpassed the 1 million unit 
mark for the first time. This period saw an 11% increase 
in passenger vehicle sales and 10% in light and medium 
commercial vehicle sales, while the heavy commercial vehicle 
segment declined by 3%.
Market performance was influenced by several factors, 
including competitive sales campaigns, macroeconomic 
uncertainties, SCT increase pushing demand forward, the 
wealth effect due to rising precious metal prices, higher 
vehicle availability, and tax incentives for electric vehicles.
During the reporting period, Ford Otosan’s domestic 
wholesale volume increased by 6% YoY to 121,655 units and 
total export volume by 11% YoY to 602,842 units. Overall, total 
sales grew by 10%, reaching an all-time high of 724,497 units.
*Total volume consists of passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles, excluding 
buses and midibuses. / Source: ODMD, TAID, and TURKSTAT.

Total Sales (Thousand Units) Domestic sales revenues decreased by 
6% YoY, amounting to TL 170,587 million 
in 2025, with a competitive pricing 
environment in the market and changes 
in product lineup leading to this decline. 
In contrast, export revenues were up by 
10%, reaching TL 660,241 million, driven by 
10% growth in exports and rise in EUR/TL 
exchange rates. As a result, total revenues 
increased by 7% YoY, amounting to TL 
830,828 million.
Sales Revenue (TL million)
In 2025, Adjusted EBITDA increased by 
19% YoY, reaching TL 66,566 million. This 
performance was influenced by several 
factors, including the increase of electric 
vehicles’ share in production, rise in 
EUR/TL exchange rates, a decrease in 
domestic sales volume, increased sales 
campaigns due to a competitive pricing 
environment, a higher proportion of 
export revenues in total revenues, and 
rising costs of imported vehicles due to 
FX activity.

(TL million)
The inflation accounting practice 
for the books kept according 
to the Tax Procedure Law was 
revoked during the reporting 
year, causing the deferred tax 
income of 2024 to turn into tax 
expenses of TL 4.4 billion in 2025. 
Additionally, the deferred tax 
income related to investment 
incentive tax assets decreased 
YoY, resulting in a net profit of TL 
33,986 million with a decline of 
33% YoY.

(TL million)
In 2025, a total gross dividend 
of TL 28 billion 555 million * 
was paid out in two separate 
installments. A dividend yield of 
7.8% was achieved, marking the 
highest level in the past 13 years. 
Including the dividend amount of 
USD 660 million paid in 2025, the 
total dividend distributed by Ford 
Otosan reached USD 6.8 billion in 
the period from 2004 to 2025.

Production, Capacity Utilization and Investments
In 2025, Türkiye’s vehicle production volume (excluding 
tractors) increased by 4% YoY to 1,419 thousand units, with 
passenger vehicle production down by 4% to 873 thousand 
units and commercial vehicle production rising by 19% 
to 547 thousand units. Meanwhile, Romania’s vehicle 
production volume declined by 3% YoY to 546 thousand 
units.
Supported by the gradual introduction of refreshed Ford 
vehicles and partner vehicle models, Ford Otosan’s total 
production increased by 11% YoY to 633 thousand units in 
2025, including 452 thousand units produced at its Turkish 
facilities and 248 thousand units at the Craiova Plant in 
Romania.
Production in Türkiye and Romania
Ford Otosan plays an increasingly critical role in Ford’s 
global electrification strategy, ad CO₂ regulations become 
stricter, compliance costs rise, and environmentally 
friendly production standards in public procurement 
become a priority. Our electrification journey, which 
started with the production of plug-in hybrid Custom in 
2019, had continued in 2022 with the launch of E-Transit, 
marking Türkiye’s and Ford’s first all-electric van. In 
2024, this transformation was further advanced with the 
introduction of new all-electric and plug-in hybrid Custom 
models.
Our production portfolio expanded with the all-electric 
versions of the Puma and Courier models launched in 
2025, accelerating our electrification journey with a new 
momentum.
Ford Otosan also manufactures six of the electric vehicle 
models that Ford plans to include in its European product 
portfolio, once again reinforcing its strategic role in the 
electric mobility transformation. Our goal is to launch 
E-Truck, the 100% electric truck, in 2026, bringing electric 
versions of all models we produce to the customers.
Ford Otosan’s production of all-electric and plug-in hybrid 
vehicles, which accounted for 5.5% of total production in 
2024 with 34,546 units, rose to 110,660 units YoY, while 
the share of electric and hybrid vehicles in our total 
production increased to 16% in 2025

Ford Otosan’s total production capacity reached 934,500 units by year-end 2025. The Yeniköy Plant 
completed the year with a capacity utilization rate of 73%, driven by the gradual increase in the production 
of 1-ton vans, currently undergoing a modernization process in Türkiye. With the completion of the 
production transition process for the new Puma Gen-E and E-Courier models, the Romania Plant closed the 
year with a capacity utilization rate of 83%.
These results show that Ford Otosan performed in parallel with both industry and European averages and 
reached a total capacity utilization rate of 75% in 2025.
In line with our strong growth and electrification plans, we invested a total of Euro 3 billion from 2021 to 
2025, including Euro 2.4 billion in Türkiye and Euro 500 million in Romania. In the same period, we created 
more than 3,000 additional jobs in Türkiye.

20         
Türkiye Automotive Market
In 2025, the Turkish automotive market achieved a new historical peak, defying 
expectations of a contraction anticipated at the beginning of the year, selling more 
than 100,000 units per month starting from March. Market growth was driven by 
several factors, including strategic sales campaigns in an intensely competitive 
pricing environment, macropolitical uncertainties, the SCT increase pushing demand 
forward, and the wealth effect due to rising gold prices. Higher vehicle availability 
and tax incentives for electric vehicles also bolstered demand.
As a result, total automotive sales in Türkiye rose by 10% YoY, reaching 1,404,135 units 
in 2025.
 (1)
Ford Otosan’s domestic retail sales increased by 3% in this period, reaching 117,048 
units and maintaining the company’s third-place position with an 8.3% market share.
The passenger vehicle segment delivered remarkable performance in 2025. 
Automobile sales surpassed 1 million units with an 11% increase, marking a historical 
first and driving growth by accounting for 77% of the total market. However, Ford 
Otosan’s sales in this segment declined by 11% to 26,454 units, primarily due to 
heightened price competition in the market and changes in SCT exemption criteria,
(2) 
resulting in a market share of 2.4%.
The light and medium commercial vehicle segments each saw a 10% increase, 
driven by higher vehicle availability and new brands entering the market. Ford 
Otosan’s sales in the light commercial vehicle segment rose by 28% to 36,297 units, 
supported by the completion of the Courier’s roll out process. Ford Otosan’s medium 
commercial vehicle sales remained similar to the previous year with 46,834 units. 
As a result, Ford Otosan claimed the leadership of the light commercial vehicle 
segment with a 24.0% market share, while reinforcing its undisputed leadership in 
the medium commercial vehicle segment with a 35.3% market share.
Heavy commercial vehicle sales decreased by 3% to 39,735 units, primarily influenced 
by uncertainties and cost pressures in key sectors such as construction and logistics. 
Ford Trucks’ domestic sales dropped by 14% to 7,463 units, resulting in a market 
share of 22.73%.
(3)
(1) Total volume consists of passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles, excluding buses and midibuses. / 
Sources: ODMD, TAID, and TURKSTAT.
(2) The upper price limit was increased from TL 1.6 million to TL 2.3 million TL, and a new mandate was introduced, 
requiring vehicles to be at least 40% localized for eligibility for exemption.
(3) Based on 16-ton and higher truck/tractor truck data. TURKSTAT data was taken as basis for non-TAİD member 
brands.

According to European Automobile Manufacturers Association (ACEA) data, the total passenger vehicle market in Europe and the UK 
expanded by 2.1%, driven by heightened competition, particularly in hybrid electric vehicle demand and the influence of Chinese brands 
despite challenging economic and geopolitical developments. The light and medium commercial vehicle market in Europe and the UK—
Ford Otosan’s largest export segment—declined by 8.5%, mainly due to the economic environment limiting fleet renewals, high base year 
effect, regulatory uncertainties related to net-zero emission targets and CO2 emissions, and rising compliance costs. As a result, the total 
European market reached 15 million units, demonstrating modest growth.
In 2025, Türkiye’s automobile exports decreased by 8.3% to 599,687 units, while commercial vehicle exports increased by 27.7% to 458,233 
units. Consequently, total automotive export volume grew by 4.4%, reaching 1,057,920 units. As a result, the value of automotive exports 
rose by 11% to USD 40.8 billion, reaffirming its position as Türkiye’s largest export sector.
In the reporting period, Ford Otosan’s total export volume rose by 10% to 602,842 units, primarily driven by the 39% YoY increase in 
1-ton van exports from Türkiye. In contrast, sales of 2-ton vans, the Courier, and the Puma, decreased by 6%, 4%, and 7%, respectively. 
Additionally, the lower demand in the European market led to a 22% decline in Ford Trucks’ exports. Overall, Ford Otosan’s total export 
revenues increased by 10% to TL 660,241 million. Ford Otosan has maintained its title as Türkiye’s goods export champion for the tenth 
consecutive year and of the Turkish automotive industry for the last 14 years.

Board of Directors’ Report
Esteemed Shareholders, Business Partners, 
and Colleagues,
We are honored to present the Board of Directors’ Report on the 
activities of Ford Otomotiv Sanayi A.Ş. for the 2025 fiscal year and 
to welcome you to our 67th Ordinary General Assembly. This report 
provides a comprehensive overview of the Company’s performance 
during the year, together with independently audited financial 
statements and accompanying notes as of December 31, 2025, as 
well as additional disclosures required under Capital Markets Board 
regulations.
Notable Developments in Türkiye and the World
The year 2025 was marked by significant economic and regulatory 
developments at both the national and global levels. In Türkiye, 
the implementation of tight monetary and fiscal policies resulted 
in gradual improvement in price stability, while high interest rates 
and exchange rate volatility continued to exert pressure on cost 
management and exporter competitiveness. At the same time, strong 
domestic demand and record sales, particularly in the automotive 
industry, supported overall economic activity.
In Europe, limited growth expectations persisted, while energy costs 
and geopolitical risks shaped the macroeconomic outlook. In this 
environment, the commercial vehicle segment contracted by 8.5%. 
The transition to electric vehicles progressed more slowly than 
anticipated, compliance costs related to net-zero and CO₂ regulations 
increased, and regulatory uncertainties continued to weigh on 
demand and industrial production. Additionally, pricing pressure 
stemming from the cost advantages of Chinese manufacturers 
prompted European producers to pursue strategic partnerships and 
collaborations aimed at optimizing costs and accelerating technology 
investments.
Globally, shifts in trade dynamics between the United States 
and China affected supply chains and international competition. 
Revisions to regulatory frameworks and uncertainties surrounding 
the implementation timeline of carbon emission and net-zero 
targets played a decisive role in the strategic planning of industrial 
and export-oriented sectors. These developments shaped the pace 
of transformation in the automotive industry and reinforced the 
importance of flexibility, cost discipline, and sustainability-oriented 
investment strategies.
Our investments in Türkiye and Romania in the past five 
years totaled Euro 3 Billion
Our total investments in Türkiye and Romania over the 2021–2025 
period amounted to Euro 3 billion. These investments enabled us 
to operate with an annual production capacity of 934,500 units. 
During this period, we accelerated our electric vehicle transformation 
projects. Following the E-Transit, PHEV Custom, and E-Transit Custom, 
we launched the E-Transit Courier and Puma Gen-E in the first 
quarter of 2025. Within the scope of the Ford–Volkswagen strategic 
alliance, we continued production of both all-electric and internal 
combustion versions of next-generation 1-ton vans at our Yeniköy 
Plant, which now has a production capacity of 405,000 units.
On January 15, 2026, we publicly disclosed that our Yeniköy Plant had 
been included in the World Economic Forum’s Global Lighthouse 
Network, of which our Gölcük Plant has been a member since 
2019. This recognition further affirmed our capabilities in advanced 
manufacturing and digital transformation. This holistic transformation 
and scalable production architecture further strengthen our strategic 
position both within the Ford world and on a global scale.
During the reporting period, we signed a Joint Cabin Development 
Agreement with Iveco, as part of our efforts toward full compliance 
with the EU’s Direct Vision (GSR-D) requirements and carbon 
reduction targets, which will come into effect in 2028. Through this 
joint engineering program, worth approximately Euro 343 million, 
we aim to leverage the power of strategic partnerships to scale up 
technology investments and improve time-to-market, with the first 
cabins planned to be ready for production by 2028.
Our Production Volume Exceeded 700,000 Units and 
Our Total Sales Surpassed 724,000 Units, Reaching the 
Highest Levels in Our History
In 2025, as a result of capacity increases, investments, and newly 
launched models, we produced 451,846 vehicles in Türkiye and 
248,328 vehicles in Romania, reaching an all-time high with a total 
production volume of 700,174 vehicles. Capacity utilization rates 
during the reporting period were 71% in Türkiye, 83% in Romania, and 
75% overall. With these figures, we accounted for 83% of commercial 
vehicle production in Türkiye and 46% of total vehicle production in 
Romania, reaffirming our industry leadership. 
The Turkish automotive market exceeded 1 million units for the third 
consecutive year, reaching 1,408,135 units in 2025 and recording 
10% year-on-year growth. This record-breaking performance was 
supported by competitive sales campaigns, changes in inflation and 
exchange rate expectations, and purchases accelerating ahead of 
increases in the Special Consumption Tax (SCT). Detailed information 
on the industry is provided on page 33 of this report. Ford Otosan 
ranked third in the domestic retail market with an 8.3% share, 
selling 117,048 vehicles. In total commercial vehicles, we maintained 
leadership with a 28.6% market share. According to ODMD data, the 
Ford brand was recognized as the “Best-Selling Light Commercial 
Vehicle Brand of the Year,” and the Courier model was named “Best-
Selling Van of the Year.”
Our primary export market, the European and UK light commercial 
vehicle market, contracted by 8.5% in 2025 due to macroeconomic 
and geopolitical uncertainties and the high base effect of the prior 
year. Ambiguities regarding regulations related to climate targets 
and the increasing costs of compliance with these targets were also 
decisive in this weak market performance. In contrast, the passenger 
vehicle market grew modestly by 2.1%, though remaining below pre-
pandemic levels.
*Total volume consists of passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles, 
excluding buses and midibuses. / Source: ODMD, TAID, and TURKSTAT

With its refreshed product portfolio, Ford Otosan produced 79% of 
the commercial vehicles Ford sold in Europe in 2025, contributing to 
Ford’s 11th consecutive year of leadership in the European commercial 
vehicle market with a record market share of 17.2%. We also 
produced the Puma, Ford’s best-selling passenger vehicle in Europe, 
accounting for 41% of that model’s European sales, supporting 
Ford’s performance in this segment. In the reporting period, total 
international sales reached 602,842 units, including 400,330 units 
exported from Türkiye and 202,512 units from Romania. Total export 
volume increased by 10%, reaching an all-time high. Ford Otosan 
maintained its position as the export leader in the Turkish automotive 
industry since 2011, accounting for 87% of Türkiye’s commercial 
vehicle exports in 2025.
Ford Otosan is the Most Valuable Automotive Company 
on Borsa Istanbul
In 2025, Ford Otosan solidified its position as the most valuable 
automotive company on Borsa Istanbul and ranked among the top 10 
most valuable companies on BIST overall, with a market cap of USD 
7.6 billion. As part of our Investor Relations program, we engaged 
with over 660 stock and bond investors and analysts through 
online meetings and at 23 conferences and roadshows in Türkiye 
and abroad, sharing updates on our company’s strategic, financial, 
operational, and sustainability performance. At Ford Otosan, we 
remain committed to enhancing the value we create through world-
class investor relations practices.
Continuing Our R&D Leadership in Product Development
As a leading product development center within the global Ford 
organization, Ford Otosan continues to drive R&D projects within the 
scope of product programs. In 2025, we invested a total of TL 12,107 
million (2024: TL 12,091 million) in R&D expenditures before project 
capitalization in 2025. After capitalization of our various product 
development projects, our R&D spending amounted to TL 8,300 
million (2024:  TL 8,076 million).
Social Responsibility Initiatives and Donations
Both as a company and as employees, we support numerous social 
responsibility projects in various areas to meet the essential needs 
of our country. Initiatives with high budgets are generally led by 
the Vehbi Koç Foundation. The total nominal amount of donations 
made to foundations, associations, and public institutions and 
organizations in 2025 was TL 559,888,364.05 (TL 562,037,542.37 
according to purchasing power as of December 31, 2025). Of this 
nominal amount, TL 501,248,235.00 was donated to the Vehbi Koç 
Foundation, TL 17,538,093.00 to Koç University, and the remaining 
TL 41,102,036.05 to other institutions and organizations, with each 
donation amounting to 4,355,414 TL or less, which are not considered 
material information for investors. The upper limit for donations in 
2026 will be determined by the general assembly.
Changes in the Board of Directors and Company 
Management
At the Ordinary General Assembly Meeting on March 26, 2025, the 
Board of Directors’ composition was set at 12 members, including 
two independent directors. The Board was elected to serve until 
the next Ordinary General Assembly, where the 2025 activities will 
be reviewed. Detailed information on changes within the Board of 
Directors and Senior Management throughout the year is available in 
the “Organizational Changes During the Reporting Year” section on 
page 107 of this report.
Ford Otosan Maintains Employment Leadership in the 
Automotive Industry
As of year-end 2025, Ford Otosan’s workforce stood at 25,002 
employees, including 19,963  hourly workers and 5,039 salaried 
employees (2024: 25,718 employees, with 20,579  hourly workers and 
5,139 salaried employees). Our hourly workers in Türkiye are covered 
under a Group Collective Labor Agreement, signed between the 
Turkish Metal Workers Union and the Turkish Employers’ Association 
of Metal Industries (MESS), which will remain in effect until August 
31, 2027.
Record Total Sales Figures Achieved in 2025 and a Strong 
Financial Outlook Maintained
While our domestic sales volume increased by 6% to 121,655 units, 
our domestic sales revenue decreased by 6% to TL 170 billion 587 
million due to competitive pricing conditions and changes in product 
mix. 
On the other hand, our international sales volume increased by 10% 
to 602,842 units, driven by the accelerated exports of the refreshed 
1-ton van models as part of the Ford and VW strategic alliance, Export 
revenues increased by 10% to TL 660 billion 241 million, supported 
by higher sales volumes and the appreciation of EUR/TL compared 
to the previous year (EUR/TL increase in 2025: 37%; EUR/TL increase 
in 2024: 13%). As a result, our total sales volume increased by 10% to 
724,497 units, while our sales revenue rose by 7% to TL 830 billion 
828 million. 
Operating profit increased by 12% to TL 42 billion 603 million, driven 
by effective cost management and the relative appreciation of the 
exchange rate, which boosted net other income. However, the rise in 
the EUR/TL exchange rate led to a 52% increase in net exchange rate 
losses reflected in financing expenses, putting pressure on pre-tax 
profit, and resulting in a 19% decrease to TL 39 billion 467 million. 
Furthermore, monetary income of TL 15 billion 873 million, due to 
declining inflation on an annual basis, contributed modestly to pre-
tax profit.
The inflation accounting implementation regarding the books kept 
according to the Tax Procedure Law was voided as of 2025, causing 
an increase in deferred tax expenses. Furthermore, the decline in 
deferred tax income related to investment incentive tax assets 
compared to the previous period led to a net profit decrease of 33% 
to TL 33 billion 986 million. The balance sheet as of December 31, 
2025 shows TL 63 billion 974 million in cash assets against TL 163 
billion 167 million in financial liabilities. We closely monitor financial 
risks and follow extremely cautious policies against such risks. We 
also take utmost care to ensure that financial assets related to risks 
remain within the basic policy limits set by the Board of Directors 
and the Early Detection and Management of Risk Committee. The 
basic policies we apply regarding various risks are explained under 
the Risk Management section on page 43 of the report, and data 
on the nature and level of risks are summarized in the notes to the 
financial statements. Total investment spending, including capitalized 
product development expenditures, new projects, and regular annual 
activities, amounted to TL 25 billion 18 million.

Our Dividend Policy and Dividend Distribution Proposal
Ford Otosan distributes dividends in compliance with the Turkish 
Commercial Code, Capital Market Regulations, Tax Regulations, and 
other applicable laws, as well as in accordance with the relevant 
article in our Articles of Incorporation. Our dividend policy aligns with 
the Corporate Governance Principles, maintaining a balanced and 
consistent approach that considers both shareholder interests and 
the company’s strategic priorities. The full text of our Dividend Policy 
is available on page 117 of this report and on our corporate website 
(www.fordotosan.com.tr). Ford Otosan, recognized as one of the most 
consistent dividend-paying companies on Borsa Istanbul, continued 
to share its profit with the shareholders even during a period of 
intense investment. In 2025, the company distributed a total of TL 27 
billion 231 million in dividends in two instalments. When adjusted for 
purchasing power as of December 31, 2025, this amount corresponds 
to TL 28 billion 555 million. Since 2004, our cumulative dividend 
distribution has reached USD 6.8 billion. In line with our Dividend 
Policy and considering our investment and financing strategies 
alongside our cash position, we propose distributing a gross cash 
dividend totaling TL 12,773,124,000 corresponding to a net ratio of 
364% (net 309.40%) to our shareholders. This equates to a gross 
dividend of TL 3.64 (net TL 3.0940) per TL 1 share, after deducting all 
legal obligations from the 2025 net profit. The proposed dividend 
distribution is scheduled to commence on 18.03.2026. For more 
information on our dividend distribution proposal, please refer to 
page 117 of this report.
Selection of the Independent Audit Firm
Pursuant to the provisions of the Turkish Commercial Code No. 6102 
and Capital Markets Law No. 6362, and upon consultation with the 
Audit Committee, the Board of Directors has resolved on February 
9
th, 2026 to appoint Güney Bağımsız Denetim ve Serbest Muhasebeci 
Mali Müşavirlik A.Ş. to conduct the independent audits on the 
Company’s financial statements and reports for the 2026 accounting 
period in addition to performing other activities within the scope of 
applicable laws and regulations. We submit this resolution to the 
General Assembly for approval.
2026 Outlook and Guidance
We have comprehensively evaluated our scenarios for 2026 and 
developed a cautious yet robust operational plan. Despite the 
volatility experienced in both the Turkish and global automotive 
industries, we aim to deliver stable performance supported by 
structural market dynamics and our refreshed product portfolio.
Türkiye’s high population, increasing mobility needs, and the aging 
vehicle fleet entering a renewal cycle have been the primary factors 
supporting the record sales observed in the automotive industry in 
recent years. Nevertheless, ongoing challenges in access to credit and 
high taxation policies continue to constrain the industry.
Following the record sales levels achieved in 2025 despite a highly 
competitive environment and volatile macroeconomic conditions, 
domestic market demand is expected to maintain its strong trajectory 
in 2026, while overall sales in the Turkish automotive market are 
projected to remain at similar levels. Progress made in combating 
inflation, the anticipated gradual decline in interest rates, ongoing 
investment expenditures, and fleet renewal activity are expected 
to continue supporting domestic market demand. However, Ford 
Otosan’s domestic sales volume is expected to be below the previous 
year’s level due to the reduction in the number of passenger vehicle 
models available in the market.
In the European market, while macroeconomic concerns and 
regulatory uncertainties persist, the normalization of economic 
activity and the low base effect are expected to support recovery, 
particularly in commercial vehicle demand. With its refreshed product 
range and multi-energy vehicle portfolio, the Ford brand is expected 
to maintain its strong position in the European market.
With the completion of the 1-ton van renewals within the scope of 
the Ford–Volkswagen strategic alliance, we anticipate an increase in 
Ford Otosan’s capacity utilization rate but foresee limited growth in 
export volumes.
In 2026, Ford Otosan aims to enhance production efficiency based 
on its quality, cost, and people-centric management approach. 
With a production capacity reaching 934,500 units and a refreshed 
production portfolio, the company seeks to further reinforce its 
position among the leading automotive manufacturers in Türkiye 
and Europe. While maintaining effective operating capital and strong 
cash management policies, the company will continue to deliver 
performance that supports profitability through a disciplined pricing 
approach and cost-reduction measures.
We extend our heartfelt gratitude to our founders, business 
partners, former and current colleagues, the Turkish Metal Union, our 
suppliers, dealers, customers, and our esteemed shareholders, whose 
invaluable contributions have been instrumental in Ford Otosan’s 
continued success.
Sincerely,
FORD OTOMOTİV SANAYİ A.Ş.
BOARD OF DIRECTORS

Chairman’s Message 
 Esteemed Stakeholders, 
We left behind a year marked by accelerated economic, technological, 
and environmental transformations across the globe, as geopolitical 
risks, regulatory changes, and fluctuating demand exerted increasing 
pressure on businesses. In such an environment, strengthening 
corporate resilience and placing long-term responsibility at the core 
of strategy have become more critical than ever.
At Ford Otosan, we enhanced the visibility of the results of our 
transformation investments in 2025, strengthened our operational 
performance, and continued to progress decisively toward the goals 
set out in our roadmap. Our technology-driven and sustainability-
focused transformation approach was embodied in an integrated 
structure encompassing all processes, from our governance model to 
our value chain.
In the automotive industry, transformation driven by electrification, 
artificial intelligence technologies, software-based solutions, and 
sustainability-focused regulations continued despite fluctuations 
in electric vehicle demand in the European market. At Ford Otosan, 
we advanced in line with our strategic priorities while effectively 
maintaining the operational agility required to adapt to evolving 
market conditions and regulatory developments.
The long-standing partnership between Ford Motor Company and 
Koç Holding has further strengthened Ford Otosan’s global role. 
In 2025, 79% of the commercial vehicles Ford sold in Europe were 
produced by Ford Otosan, clear evidence of our product development 
capabilities, flexible production structure, and operational excellence. 
This performance reflects strong and sustainable value creation, as 
demonstrated by Ford’s 11-year leadership and 17.2% market share in 
the European commercial vehicle market.
In line with our electrification strategy, we commenced serial 
production of the E-Transit Courier, E-Tourneo Courier, and Puma 
Gen-E in 2025. With the addition of the E-Transit, E-Transit Custom, 
and E-Tourneo Custom, our product portfolio evolved into a more 
balanced and comprehensive lineup. We plan to initiate electrification 
investments in the heavy commercial vehicle segment in 2026. Our 
ability to manufacture internal combustion and electric models 
on the same production lines at our Kocaeli and Craiova facilities 
remains one of the strongest indicators of our flexible, multi-energy 
production capability and our capacity to respond swiftly to changing 
demand patterns.
The integration between our operations in Türkiye and Romania 
continues to strengthen our global production capabilities. The 
addition of the Romanian plant to our operational footprint has 
enabled us to produce a broad range of products, from passenger 
vehicles to heavy commercial vehicles. Today, Ford’s 41% of passenger 
vehicles sales in Europe are produced by Ford Otosan. Demonstrating 
the depth of integration we have achieved.
During the 2021–2025 investment period, we invested a total of Euro 
3 billion in Türkiye and Romania. Within this scope, the restructuring 
of the Yeniköy Plant, one of the largest investments undertaken 
by the Turkish private sector, represented a significant milestone. 
The facility, which has become the sole production center for the 
Transit Custom family and next-generation 1-ton vans under the 
Ford-Volkswagen alliance, was included in the World Economic 
Forum’s Global Lighthouse Network thanks to the digitalization 
and sustainability practices implemented under the “Factory of the 
Future” vision.
Looking at our financial performance, we reached USD 19.4 billion in 
total revenues in 2025. Exports from Türkiye totaled 400,330 units, 
generating USD 11.5 billion in revenue. With the contribution of our 
Romanian operations, total international sales reached 602,842 
units, generating USD 15.4 billion in revenue. Our export leadership 
was reaffirmed, accounting for 38% of Türkiye’s total vehicle exports 
and 87% of commercial vehicle exports. In 2025, we also distributed 
a total of TL 27 billion in dividends to our shareholders in two 
installments.
Sustainability has become an integral component of our business 
model. Activities carried out in alignment with Ford Motor Company 
and Koç Holding have enabled us to lead the sustainability 
transformation of the Turkish automotive supply chain. Reducing 
environmental impact, strengthening our social impact capacity, 
and maximizing compliance across all processes remain our core 
priorities.
In all our social investments, we uphold the legacy of the late Vehbi 
Koç, founder of the Koç Group, who said, “I exist if my country exists.” 
In 2025, the support extended to the Vehbi Koç Foundation in the 
amount of TL 501 million contributed significantly to in the fields of 
education, culture, and health. With the new school investments in 
Kocaeli, the number of educational institutions established in the 
region will reach five. Our long-term social support programs for the 
earthquake-affected region have reached more than 25,000 people, 
and the “My Dream Future” program has engaged with 110,000 
women in technology and innovation.
The year 2026 holds special significance for us as it marks the 100th 
anniversary of the founding of the Koç Group. Growing alongside 
the Republic, Koç Group has played a pioneering role in Türkiye’s 
economic development through its investments in industry and 
services, and in societal development through its social and cultural 
initiatives.

Ford Otosan Leader’s Message 
Esteemed Stakeholders, 
The automotive industry today represents far more than vehicle 
design and manufacturing; it has evolved into a comprehensive 
mobility ecosystem shaping freedom of movement, urban 
dynamics, and business efficiency. Digitalization, electrification, 
and sustainability continue to transform not only the quality of our 
products but also the speed of our processes and the structure of our 
value chain. This transformation is not merely changing the rules of 
the game; it is redefining them.
For Ford Otosan, 2025 was not a transitional year, but one in which 
the results of our strategic decisions became clearly visible. Following 
our “Future. Now.” vision, we strengthened our operational flexibility, 
maintained discipline in commercial negotiations, deepened 
customer loyalty, and reinforced stakeholder trust. Rather than 
adapting to change, we continued to lead it through execution.
The Turkish automotive market required careful consideration 
throughout the year. Cautious sentiment at the beginning of the 
year shifted toward dynamic demand, driven by rising inflation 
and exchange rate expectations, as well as the impact of Special 
Consumption Tax (SCT) regulations. The total market reached 
approximately 1.4 million units across all segments. Despite limited 
loan availability and a higher tax burden compared to Europe, this 
performance once again demonstrated the vitality and potential of 
the Turkish automotive industry.
In Europe, however, a different environment prevailed. Intensified 
global competition, slower-than-expected progress in electrification, 
and evolving regulatory frameworks weakened demand. These 
developments underscored that competition is no longer defined 
solely by volume, but increasingly by flexibility, cost discipline, 
quality, and the ability to manage multiple technologies 
simultaneously.
In this environment, our flexibility and multi-technology, multi-energy 
production capability became a decisive strategic advantage in 
2025. We managed our total capacity of 934,500 units across Türkiye 
and Romania efficiently and in a balanced manner despite market 
volatility. Our ability to manufacture internal combustion, hybrid, and 
electric vehicles on the same production lines enabled us to build 
a resilient operational model capable of adapting rapidly to shifting 
demand. As a result, we maintained our leadership in Türkiye’s 
commercial vehicle market and contributed significantly to Ford’s 
strong position in Europe.
In 2025, we manufactured 700,174 vehicles and achieved total sales 
of 724,497 units, including 121,655 units in the domestic market and 
602,842 units in international markets, thanks to our high performing 
production system. Our focus on flexibility, quality, and speed 
enabled us to preserve competitiveness under changing market 
conditions.
We supported our operational performance with disciplined financial 
management centered on long-term value creation. In 2025, our 
revenues reached approximately USD 19.4 billion. Our transparent 
capital markets approach and strong corporate governance were 
reaffirmed with BB+ and BB- credit ratings by Fitch Ratings and S&P 
Global, respectively. These outcomes reflect our financial resilience 
amid heightened global uncertainty.
As we monitored sustainability-related regulations closely, we 
treated this framework not merely as a compliance requirement but 
as a strategic compass guiding our competitiveness. We proactively 
transformed both our operations and our broader ecosystem 
accordingly.
In 2025, we published our first report compliant with the Turkish 
Sustainability Reporting Standards (TSRS). Prepared in alignment 
with International Financial Reporting Standards (IFRS) developed 
by the International Sustainability Standards Board (ISSB), the report 
enables us to present climate- and sustainability-related risks and 
opportunities within a structured financial reporting framework.
We also finalized our 2050 net-zero roadmap following approval of 
our targets by the Science Based Targets initiative (SBTi). Through 
updated climate and water scenario analyses, we assessed transition 
and physical risks comprehensively. We sourced 100% of our 
electricity from renewable energy across all campuses in Türkiye and 
Romania and continued our solar energy investments. Our AI-based 
energy management project marked an important step in advancing 
energy efficiency and reducing emissions.
This integrated approach was reflected in our corporate reputation 
and stakeholder recognition. In 2025, our efforts were acknowledged 
by the Kocaeli Chamber of Industry, KalDer, TKSD, and the Sustainable 
Business Awards. We were recognized as an Industry Low Carbon 
Leader by Sustainalytics. We maintained our position as Türkiye’s 
first automotive company in the Bloomberg Gender-Equality Index 
and continued to be listed in the LSEG Global Diversity and Inclusion 
Index Top 100. Additionally, we ranked among the top 50 companies 
worldwide in the Global Inclusion Index 2025.
Our Yeniköy Plant further strengthened its role as a strategic hub 
within Ford Otosan and Ford’s transformation journey in Europe. 
We successfully launched next-generation 1-ton van models 
developed in collaboration with Ford and Volkswagen. At our Craiova 
Plant, we advanced our electrification strategy by commencing 
serial production of the all-electric Puma and Courier models. The 
inclusion of our Yeniköy Plant in the World Economic Forum’s Global 
Lighthouse Network at the beginning of 2026 further validated our 
leadership in digital transformation.

Our Ford Trucks business continued to expand internationally,

In 2025, we also identified supply chain transformation as a strategic 
priority. Guided by our ethical principles, we integrated performance 
and sustainability criteria more systematically into our processes.

technical development training to our suppliers. In addition, we 
assessed the performance of 286 suppliers and conducted on-site

STEM awareness from an early age including girls in the target 
group. With the science initiatives implemented in schools, we aim 
to reach 40,000 girls across Türkiye by the first half of 2026. Through 
employment practices supporting neurodiverse and disabled 
individuals, we continued to foster an inclusive and accessible 
workplace for all employees.
These results demonstrate Ford Otosan’s consistent ability to create 
value under evolving conditions. I extend my sincere thanks to all 
our colleagues and stakeholders whose dedication made these 
achievements possible.

Automotive Industry Overview
Global Developments
Globally, light vehicle sales are expected to reach approximately 91.7 million units in 2025. This 
performance marks the first time in the post-pandemic period that sales have exceeded the 89.9 million 
units recorded in 2019, indicating that the global market has entered a phase of gradual normalization 
following the disruptions caused by the pandemic and the supply chain constraints that persisted from 
2020 through early 2024.
1 Within this broader recovery, global electric vehicle sales also reached a new 
record, with sales estimated at around 20.7 million units, representing approximately 20% year-on-year 
growth.
2
China maintained its position as the world’s largest new electric vehicle market for the 11th consecutive 
year in 2025,3 while in Europe, high cost pressures, strict CO₂ targets, and new tariff measures were 
among the key factors shaping production and demand balances. Despite initial pressures from high 
vehicle prices and elevated credit costs, the North American (US) market closed the year with modest 
growth of 2.3%, reaching 16.3 million units, while the Japanese market recorded growth of 3.26%. 
Looking ahead, the overall outlook suggests that global sales will remain broadly stable at around 
91.86 million units in 2026, reflecting limited growth of approximately 0.2%, with market dynamics 
increasingly shaped by regional policy frameworks, cost pressures, and the pace of electrification.
1
The EU and the UK - Developments
In 2025, the European automotive market displayed diverging dynamics across vehicle segments. Across 
the European Union, new passenger vehicle registrations increased by 1.8% to 10.8 million units, while 
registrations of light commercial vehicles declined by 8.8% and heavy commercial vehicles by 6.2%. In 
France, one of the region’s largest markets, passenger vehicle registrations decreased by 5.0%, while 
light commercial vehicle registrations fell by 5.6% and heavy commercial vehicles by 9.0%. In Germany, 
passenger vehicle registrations increased slightly by 1.4% to 2.9 million units, whereas light commercial 
vehicle registrations declined by 5.4% and heavy commercial vehicles by 12.2%. In the United Kingdom, 
passenger vehicle registrations increased by 3.5% to 2,020,523 units
4 while light commercial vehicle 
registrations declined by 10.3% to 315,846 units. Heavy commercial vehicle registrations recorded modest 
growth of 0.65%.
5
Persistently high interest rates, combined with weaker industrial activity in major economies such as 
Germany and France, have been among the key structural factors shaping segment-specific market 
dynamics and overall demand. The slowdown in industrial production across the euro area has 
particularly affected fleet renewal cycles and investment decisions in commercial transport, contributing 
to the contraction observed in the commercial vehicle segment.
6 More broadly, weak growth 
expectations, elevated energy costs, and ongoing geopolitical risks continued to weigh on the European 
automotive industry. At the same time, the slower-than-expected pace of electric vehicle adoption and 
the rising cost of regulatory compliance placed additional pressure on manufacturers, particularly during 
model transition cycles.
6
Public procurement preferences for environmentally friendly production standards also reshaped 
demand, particularly for product groups that are not fully compliant with regulations or are in the 
technological transition phase. These factors have influenced both production planning and inventory 
management, ultimately shaping overall vehicle registration volumes across the region.
7  
China – Developments
In 2025, China’s automotive industry reached a new record level, with production totaling 34.5 million 
units and sales reaching 34.4 million units. Sales increased by 9.4% year-on-year, allowing China to 
maintain its position as the world’s largest automobile market for the 17
th consecutive year, while 
production and sales volumes remained above the 30-million-unit threshold for the third consecutive 
year. The acceleration of new model launches by manufacturers, combined with government incentives 
supporting vehicle replacement, stimulated domestic demand and led to year-end results that exceeded 
expectations. Exports also emerged as one of the strongest growth drivers for China’s automotive 
industry in 2025. Total vehicle exports reached a record 7.09 million units, representing an increase of 
21.1%. As Chinese manufacturers expanded their global presence and strengthened their competitiveness 
in international markets, exports of new energy vehicles (NEVs) doubled year-on-year to 2.6 million 
units. Industry concentration also remained notable: the top 15 automotive groups accounted for 
92.3% of total sales, highlighting the importance of economies of scale and consolidation in shaping 
competition within the sector.
8
1S&P Global, 2025 automotive sales data highlights mixed global trends
2Benchmark Mineral Intelligence, Global EV sales reach 20.7 million units in 2025, growing by 20%
3Nasdaq, China Auto Market Boomed in 2025: Why Growth May Be Softer in 2026 (press release)
4European Automobile Manufacturers’ Association (ACEA) – 2025 PC Report.
5European Automobile Manufacturers’ Association (ACEA) – 2025 CV Report.
6Eurostat, Industrial production down by 1.2% in the euro area and by 1.0% in the EU
7European Commission, A plan for EU competitiveness and decarbonisation
8Gasgoo, China’s 2025 auto market hits new highs in both annual sales, output

Automotive Industry Overview
China – Developments 
In terms of technology adoption, the market has undergone a strategic shift influenced by charging 
infrastructure limitations and consumer concerns regarding driving range. Alongside fully electric 
vehicles, plug-in hybrid (PHEV) and extended-range electric vehicle (EREV) technologies, which offer 
greater flexibility through hybrid refueling solutions, have gained increasing traction. At the same 
time, the rapid expansion of autonomous driving features, connectivity technologies, and intelligent 
cockpit systems has accelerated the diffusion of advanced vehicle technologies across price segments. 
As consumers become more familiar with these capabilities, manufacturers increasingly differentiate 
their products through scenario-based digital features and user-experience-focused mobility services, 
intensifying competition in the Chinese market.
9
Türkiye - Developments 
The Turkish automotive market reached a historic record level in 2025, with total sales of 1,408,135* 
units, representing approximately 10% growth compared to the previous year. The combined market for 
passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles increased by 10.49% to 1,368,400 units. An analysis 
of the key drivers behind this performance indicates that intense price competition among brands 
seeking to maintain market share, combined with aggressive sales campaigns, played a decisive role in 
supporting demand. In addition, expectations regarding inflation and exchange rate volatility, together 
with anticipated increases in Special Consumption Tax (SCT) rates, led consumers to bring forward 
purchasing decisions, contributing significantly to the strong sales performance.
10
In passenger vehicles, sales increased by 10.62% to 1,084,496 units, with imports accounting for 70% of 
this segment. A notable shift was observed in fuel preferences: gasoline vehicles held the largest share 
at 47%, while hybrid vehicles reached 27.2% and fully electric vehicles accounted for 17.7% of the market. 
During this period, the combined share of electric and hybrid vehicles significantly exceeded that of 
diesel vehicles, which declined to 7.4%.
10 In the commercial vehicle segment, performance varied across 
subsegments. The light commercial vehicle market reached 283,904 units in 2025, representing a 10% 
increase, with imports accounting for 78% of the segment. By contrast, the heavy commercial vehicle 
market declined by 4%, falling to 45,503 units. This contraction was primarily driven by decreases of 4% 
in the truck market and 3% in the midibus market, while the bus segment recorded modest growth of 
2%.
11
Meanwhile, the industry’s total production increased by 4% in 2025, reaching 1,419,464 units. A 
significant distinction was observed between segments. Commercial vehicle production increased 
by 19% overall, while passenger vehicle production decreased by 3.5% to 872,538 units. This strong 
performance in the commercial vehicle group was particularly supported by increases in minibus (66%) 
and light commercial vehicle (13%) production. Meanwhile, total automotive production increased by 
4% in 2025, reaching 1,419,464 units. A clear divergence was observed between segments. Commercial 
vehicle production increased by 19%, while passenger vehicle production declined by 3.5% to 872,538 
units. This strong performance in the commercial vehicle segment was particularly supported by 
increases in minibus production (66%) and light commercial vehicle production (13%). On the foreign 
trade side, total automotive exports increased by 4% in volume, reaching 1,057,920 units, with passenger 
vehicles accounting for 75% of exports. The commercial vehicle segment stood out with 28% growth 
in export volumes, while passenger vehicle exports declined by 8% compared to the previous year. In 
monetary terms, total automotive exports increased by 11% to USD 40.8 billion, enabling the automotive 
industry to maintain its position as Türkiye’s leading export sector.
11
* Total volume consists of passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles, excluding buses and midibuses. / Sources: ODMD, TAID, and TURKSTAT.
9McKinsey & Company, China Auto Consumer Insights 2025: Gaining momentum
10Automotive Distributors and Mobility Association (ODMD), Press Release, January 2026

Developments Impacting the Industry
The year 2025 marked a strategic transition period for the global automotive ecosystem, characterized 
by both increasing regulatory requirements and growing calls from industry stakeholders for greater 
implementation flexibility. The European Green Deal, which aims to transform the European Union 
into a modern, resource-efficient, and competitive economy, mandates a reduction of at least 55% 
in greenhouse gas emissions by 2030, alongside longer-term targets of 90% by 2040 and climate 
neutrality by 2050, as established under the European Climate Law. Within this framework, the Carbon 
Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM), designed to encourage lower-carbon production globally, is 
expected to become fully operational in 2026.
12 However, this ambitious transformation is unfolding 
during a period of heightened geopolitical and economic uncertainty. The World Economic Forum 
(WEF) Global Risks Report 2026 identifies an “Age of Competition” defined by intense geopolitical, 
geoeconomic, and technological rivalry, in which trade, finance, and technology increasingly function as 
strategic policy instruments. In this context, maintaining industrial competitiveness and supply chain 
resilience while advancing sustainability goals has emerged as a key priority for policymakers and 
industry alike.
13 Reflecting this need for balance, the European Commission’s “Omnibus I” package seeks 
to simplify certain regulatory requirements related to the Corporate Sustainability Reporting Directive 
(CSRD) and the Corporate Sustainability Due Diligence Directive (CSDDD). The proposed adjustments 
aim to reduce administrative complexity, improve the practicality of reporting obligations, and provide 
greater clarity regarding implementation timelines. Among the proposed changes are a narrowing of 
the reporting scope, more flexible implementation schedules for certain companies, and a stronger 
emphasis on standardized and data-driven reporting frameworks. At the same time, discussions around 
the CSDDD have increasingly focused on concentrating obligations on the largest global companies in 
order to ensure proportional implementation.
14
The European Commission has also introduced additional flexibility in the implementation of CO₂ 
emission standards for passenger vehicles and light commercial vehicles. Instead of assessing 
compliance solely on an annual basis beginning in 2025, the Commission has proposed evaluating 
compliance over a three-year average covering the period 2025–2027. Under current fleet emission 
targets, manufacturers may need to achieve a significant share of battery electric vehicle sales to meet 
fleet CO₂ requirements and avoid potential penalties.
15 However, a joint statement by ACEA and CLEPA 
highlights that several structural challenges continue to affect the pace of electrification in Europe. 
These include the region’s dependence on external suppliers for key battery value-chain components, 
the uneven expansion of charging infrastructure, and relatively high electricity prices.
As a result, electric vehicle market shares in Europe remain below levels required for rapid 
decarbonization, with approximately 15% for passenger vehicles, 9% for light commercial vehicles, and 
3.5% for heavy commercial vehicles. Industry organizations therefore argue that the transition should 
incorporate a technology-neutral approach, considering a broader range of solutions, including hybrids, 
range extenders, hydrogen technologies, and low-carbon fuels, alongside battery electric vehicles.
16
Despite these calls for flexibility, global climate policy momentum continues to intensify. Discussions 
at the COP30 summit in Belém highlighted the role of climate finance and alternative fuel pathways 
in accelerating decarbonization in the transportation sector. The concept of technology neutrality 
was again emphasized in policy discussions, encouraging the consideration of multiple low-emission 
technologies, including electric, hydrogen, and sustainable fuels, as complementary pathways toward 
emission reduction targets.
17 Within the European regulatory landscape, additional legislation is also 
shaping the industry’s transition toward more transparent and sustainable supply chains. The EU 
Deforestation Regulation (EUDR), whose implementation timeline has been extended to December 
2026 for large companies to allow additional time for compliance systems and digital traceability 
infrastructure, introduces new due diligence requirements for companies operating in global commodity 
supply chains.
18 Meanwhile, the EU Battery Regulation, postponed to August 2027, establishes a 
framework for battery sustainability, recycling, and traceability, including due diligence requirements 
that are expected to be phased in gradually over the coming years.
19
In Türkiye, regulatory developments are also supporting the automotive industry’s environmental 
transition. With Presidential Decree No. 10115, published in the Official Gazette on July 24, 2025, the 
Special Consumption Tax (SCT) system for passenger vehicles was restructured, introducing a tiered 
taxation structure based on engine power and the vehicle’s tax-exclusive price.
20 At the same time, 
industrial policies aligned with the Medium Term Program (2026–2028) aim to support technological 
transformation and environmental compliance across key sectors. The HIT-30 High Technology 
Investment Program encourages domestic production of critical technologies, while the Net Zero 
Emission Strategy and Action Plan in Transportation supports investments in high-capacity charging 
infrastructure and the development of a hydrogen value chain within logistics networks.
21
12European Commission, The European Green Deal Striving to be the first climate-neutral continent

Developments Impacting the Industry
Deepening Tariff and Resource Tensions in Global Competition
The global automotive industry is entering a new phase of competition shaped by the rapid export 
expansion and cost advantages of Chinese manufacturers. China’s vehicle exports reached 4.95 million 
units by the third quarter of 2025, representing an increase of 14.8% year-on-year. Electric vehicles 
accounted for 1.76 million units of these exports, already surpassing the total figures recorded in the 
previous year.
22 At the same time, the adoption of electric vehicles in China’s domestic market has 
accelerated significantly.23 Electric vehicles accounted for approximately half of new vehicle sales in 
certain months of 2025, reflecting the rapid expansion of the country’s new energy vehicle ecosystem. 
China’s share of global automotive production capacity has also increased markedly, rising from around 
10% in 2010 to approximately 40% today, highlighting the structural shift of manufacturing capacity 
toward Asia. According to the International Energy Agency, the production cost of a Chinese-made 
electric vehicle can be around 30% lower than comparable models produced in Europe or the United 
States, largely due to strong vertical integration across the battery and supply chain ecosystem.
24
In response to these developments, the European Union launched an investigation to determine 
whether electric vehicles imported from China benefited from unfair price advantages linked to state 
subsidies.
23 Following this investigation, the EU introduced countervailing duties ranging from 8% to 
35.3%, in addition to the existing 10% customs duty applied to imported vehicles. On the consumer side, 
the pace of electrification continues to vary considerably across regions. Norway remains the global 
leader, with electric vehicles accounting for more than 80% of new vehicle sales, while adoption in 
the United States remains relatively limited at around 7–8% of the market. Survey data also suggests 
that vehicle affordability and charging infrastructure availability remain key barriers to wider adoption. 
Approximately 60% of consumers consider battery electric vehicles too expensive, while 54% cite 
insufficient charging infrastructure as a major obstacle.
22
In Türkiye, several policy measures have been introduced in recent years to protect domestic production 
and address current account balance. A Presidential Decree issued in June 2024 imposed an additional 
40% customs duty on passenger vehicles of Chinese origin.
25 This protectionist framework was further 
expanded with a Presidential Decree published in September 2025, introducing additional financial 
obligations on passenger vehicle imports from the European Union and other countries without Free 
Trade Agreements, with rates varying according to engine type.
Under the new regulation, an additional financial obligation of 25% applies to conventional and hybrid 
vehicles, while electric and plug-in hybrid vehicles are subject to a 30% rate. 26 These regulatory 
developments reflect Türkiye’s efforts to protect domestic automotive production and safeguard 
ongoing investment in the national automotive ecosystem amid an increasingly competitive global trade 
environment.27
Global Semi-conductor Crisis
In the global automotive industry, geopolitical uncertainties surrounding the supply of semiconductor 
components have emerged as a strategic risk area. While the industry has increasingly focused on 
advanced semiconductors, supply constraints in legacy chips used in vehicle control systems and 
power electronics continue to pose challenges. Combined with trade restrictions and limited supplier 
diversification, these factors have heightened concerns regarding supply security. At the same time, 
the industry’s reliance on just-in-time inventory practices has increased the vulnerability of production 
networks to concentrated supply disruptions.
28 As a result, capacity constraints in critical components 
can rapidly affect production lines and lead to temporary assembly stoppages. For the European 
automotive industry in particular, uncertainties in semiconductor supply continue to affect short-term 
production planning. In response, manufacturers are increasingly seeking to diversify supplier networks, 
strengthen strategic partnerships, and support new semiconductor manufacturing capacity, particularly 
for essential automotive-grade components.
29
22Automobility Ltd., State of China’s Auto Market Report, October 2025.

Corporate Governance
Board of Directors and its Functions
Ford Otosan’s Board of Directors establishes a governance framework pursuant to the Turkish 
Commercial Code, Capital Market Board regulations, and the provisions of the Articles of Incorporation to 
ensure the company’s long-term success, sustainable value creation, and stakeholder protection goals. 
The Board of Directors is responsible for the strategic planning and oversight of all company operations, 
without prejudice to the non-transferable powers of the General Assembly.
The Board of Directors’ key duties include approving company strategies, annual business plans and 
budgets, appointing senior management and evaluating their performance, making major investment 
and financing decisions, and reviewing and approving financial statements. The Board of Directors also 
oversees the effectiveness of risk management, internal control, and compliance processes, and ensures 
that sustainability, ethics management, and stakeholder relations are embedded into the corporate 
structure.
Board of Directors’ Composition
Ford Otosan’s Board of Directors consists of 12 members, including 2 independent directors. The 
Chairman of the Board is a non-executive but non-independent member, and the duties of Chairman 
and Ford Otosan Leader (CEO) are held by different individuals. Board members possess extensive 
experience in automotive operations, finance, risk management, engineering, international markets, 
manufacturing, supply chain, capital markets, information security, sustainability, and technology. 
The system is designed to support the company’s expanding operations, innovation agenda, and 
electrification, digitalization, and sustainability transition.
Nomination and Diversity 
During the nomination process for Board members, we consider the knowledge, expertise and 
competencies required for the position in accordance with the principles stated in our Board of Directors 
Diversity Policy, and we prioritize having a Board composed of members with diverse experiences 
and skills. We also aim to achieve diversity in terms of age, gender, race, nationality, and ethnicity by 
focusing on key areas such as inclusion, gender equality and equal pay for equal work, which are all 
emphasized in our policy. We believe that diversity on the Board of Directors is a key factor for elevating 
the company’s success in the eyes of its shareholders and other stakeholders.
To view Board of Directors Diversity Policy, please click 
herehere.

Independence Principles
Independent directors play a critical role in contributing to the Board’s objectivity and balanced 
decision-making. Each year, independent directors submit their declarations of independence according 
to the Capital Markets Board’s Corporate Governance Principles. The Corporate Governance Committee 
evaluates these declarations and presents its opinion to the Board of Directors. Pursuant to the Articles 
of Incorporation, the Board of Directors is required to have at least two independent directors, selected 
from among candidates nominated by Group B and Group C shareholders. The external duties of Board 
members are regularly monitored to ensure they allocate sufficient time to company business and 
actively participate in the meetings.
Please click here to view the Board members’ resumes.
For more information on the Board of Directors candidates, please click here.
Meeting Structure and Frequency
The Board of Directors performs its duties in accordance with the procedures defined in the Articles 
of Incorporation and the Capital Markets Board regulations. In 2025, the Board of Directors convened 
15 times to regularly and effectively oversee the company’s strategic direction, financial performance, 
operational priorities, and sustainability agenda.

value creation model included:
• Business Area Assessments
• New Project and Transformation Opportunities
• Investment Approvals

Corporate Governance
We adopt a corporate governance approach that focuses on responsibility, fairness, transparency and 
accountability for the entire Ford Otosan ecosystem, starting with the stakeholders and shareholders. 
Our governance model, which considers the interests and benefits of all stakeholders to ensure 
the continuity of our robust financial performance, forms the foundation of our business processes, 
providing a framework for strengthening the value creation initiatives.
The accuracy and transparency of our financial and nonfinancial data is ensured through independent 
audit processes. Independent audit firms apply various auditing techniques such as reconciliation, 
invoice checks, analytical controls and recalculations in accordance with national and international 
standards, including IFRS, TFRS, TSRS, IAS and BDS. We review the audit results based on reports 
prepared according to BDS and address key audit issues in these reports. We apply the findings after 
making the necessary corrections to the financial and non-financial data and present them with 
comparisons of at least two reporting periods.
We present the calculation methods of the metrics that have passed the verification in detail in our 
reports and clearly indicate that the verification process has been completed by adding a green check 
mark next to these metrics in the relevant tables. Additionally, we disclose the difficulties experienced 
during the audit process and recommendations for the relevant departments with the executive 
summary and ensure that remedial actions are taken. These comprehensive processes enable us to 
strengthen our corporate governance approach as we maintain a sustainable and transparent reporting 
approach.
More information is provided under the Corporate Governance Principles Compliance Report section.
Senior Management
Senior Management Structure
The company’s senior management operates with 15 executives, including five women. The senior 
management roles consist of: the General Manager, Assistant General Managers, and directors 
reporting directly to the General Manager and the Deputy General Manager. The senior management 
is responsible for the following business areas: Legal and Compliance, Supply Chain, Corporate 
Communications and Sustainability, Commercial Vehicles, Product Development, Ford Trucks Business 
Area, Growth and Smart Mobility, Finance, Digital Products and Services, Human Resources and 
Transformation, Ford Türkiye Business Area, Quality, New Projects and Investments.
During the reporting period, our Corporate 
Governance Rating rose from 9.57 to 9.60 (out of 10).
