Compliance Program
At Ford Otosan, we comply with applicable legislation, contractual requirements, organizational 
standards such as policies and procedures, ethical principles and voluntary compliance commitments 
in line with our principles of integrity, honesty, respect, accountability and transparency. Therefore, 
we strive to fulfill our compliance obligations to the maximum, identify and prevent the related risks 
and implement corrective measures. Accordingly, we have run a comprehensive Compliance Program 
since 2021, while reviewing and expanding the relevant policies. Through these policies, approved by 
the Board of Directors, we aim to provide guidance for all Ford Otosan stakeholders. The Compliance 
Program is implemented by the Compliance Leadership and Compliance Officers under the Ford Otosan 
Legal and Compliance Leadership, which reports directly to the Ford Otosan Leader and indirectly 
to the Board of Directors through the Early Determination and Management of Risk Committee. The 
Compliance Committee is tasked with ensuring alignment with the Compliance Program on the Ford 
Otosan senior management level. The Compliance Committee, consisting of the Legal and Compliance 
Leader, Ford Otosan Leader, CFO, Human Resources and Transformation Leader and other department 
leaders as needed, functions as an advisory board that supports the Legal and Compliance Leader in the 
decision-making process when required. On the other hand, Ford Otosan senior management serves as 
a role model for the entire organization and leads all employees by example to instill compliance as a 
part of Ford Otosan culture and ingrain it into the attitudes and behaviors of employees. Ford Otosan’s 
Compliance Program is built on three pillars: Prevention, Identification, Response. These are shown in 
detail in the chart below.
Since upholding human rights is a significant compliance risk, Ford Otosan conducts a Human Rights 
Due Diligence study every two years, engaging with the employees and suppliers to strengthen its 
ethics culture and approach to respecting human rights. In 2025, the necessary activities were carried 
out, surveys and workshops were conducted, the human rights risk inventory was updated, and areas 
for action were determined.
To comply with the latest updates to the Law on the Protection of Personal Data, we carried out 
extensive work within the organization. In operations that involve regular personal data transfer abroad, 
business partners who are required to sign standard personal data privacy agreements, as defined by 
the Personal Data Protection Authority, were identified. Agreements were signed with 25 of the partners 
and submitted to the Authority.
For our Compliance Policy and other compliance policies, please click here.

Risk Management
RISK MANAGEMENT AND RISK MANAGEMENT 
COMMITTEE’S ACTIVITIES
Ford Otosan adopts a risk management approach designed to anticipate, manage, and monitor potential 
risks that may affect the Company’s stakeholders - primarily shareholders – in alignment with corporate 
strategies and objectives. This approach also involves proactively developing action plans for risk and 
crisis management.
Following the Board of Directors’ resolution dated July 10, 2012, in accordance with Article 378 of the 
Turkish Commercial Code and the Capital Markets Board’s Corporate Governance Communiqué, the Early 
Detection and Management of Risk Committee was established to ensure the effective functioning of 
board committees. The Committee is responsible for the early identification of risks that may jeopardize 
the Company’s existence, development, and continuity, the implementation of necessary measures, 
and the effective management of these risks. The Committee is chaired by independent Board member 
Umran Savaş İnan, with Board members Haydar Yenigün and Dave Johnston serving as members.
The Committee, which convened six times in 2025, evaluates the principles of the Ford Otomotiv 
A.Ş. Risk Management System and related reporting processes, reviews periodic Risk Reports, and 
provides recommendations on actions to be taken in cases where defined risk limits are exceeded. 
The Committee also reviews the effectiveness of the Risk Management System and monitors the 
implementation of actions across relevant departments in line with its decisions. In addition, it assesses 
risks related to information technologies, cybersecurity, and IT suppliers, monitors compliance risks 
and related actions, and evaluates sustainability-related risks. Reporting activities and Committee 
evaluations are regularly submitted to the Board of Directors.
Activities related to the identification, management, and monitoring of strategic, operational, financial, 
legal compliance, technology and innovation, ESG (environmental, social, and governance), and external 
risks are carried out by the Corporate Risk Management Leadership, which reports directly to the Finance 
Platform Area Leadership, and are reported to the Risk Committee. The Internal Audit Leadership, 
reporting directly to the Ford Otosan Leader, and the Internal Control Leadership, reporting directly to 
the Finance Platform Area Leadership, regularly submit their reports on internal control and internal 
audit activities to the Audit Committee. All three departments are functionally independent of one 
another.
CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
Risk management activities, steered by the Corporate Risk Management leadership, encompass the 
entire organization. They are integrated into all business processes and carried out through designated 
risk coordinators from each department. Emerging risks and opportunities are proactively monitored, 
and their potential long-term impact on Ford Otosan is evaluated. The outcomes are regularly 
communicated to senior management and the Risk Committee.
The risk management approach is designed to foster a common risk culture across the organization 
from bottom to top. In 2025, senior management, leaders, and department risk coordinators received 
a total of 904 person-hours of interactive risk management training. As part of awareness efforts, a 
risk email address is used for direct communication of risk-related matters and ensuring the active 
participation of all employees in improving risk management systems.
Ford Otosan categorizes risks under the main topics of operational, financial, legal compliance, ESG, 
technology and innovation, and external risks. Within this risk management system, aligned with the 
ISO 31000 risk management standard, risks are systematically identified, root cause and impact analyses 
are conducted, controls are defined, and mitigation actions are planned.

CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
Departmental risks are reviewed with senior management, risk coordinators, and department leaders to 
embed risk management into the company culture. Risks prioritized in line with corporate strategies are 
then reviewed with Ford Otosan Leaders, including senior management, and are regularly reported to 
the Risk Committee and incorporated into decision-making processes.
In its operations, Ford Otosan supports the sustainable development of society, contributes to social 
well-being, and takes into account the needs of future generations, while addressing environmental, 
social, and governance (ESG) risks. Topics such as climate change, environmental impacts, employee 
engagement and development, equity, diversity and inclusion, human rights, talent retention, supply 
chain, cybersecurity, and legal compliance are assessed comprehensively. The identified risks and 
mitigation actions are managed concurrently and reported regularly to the Risk Committee. To 
strengthen accountability, ESG risks are also included in senior management performance criteria.
Ford Otosan identifies and manages occupational health and safety (OHS) risks through OHS committees 
established in line with ISO 45001. These risks are reviewed through defined targets and structured 
programs, and are regularly reported to and evaluated by the Risk Committee.
Company-wide risks consolidated by the Corporate Risk Management team, prioritized in 2025, and 
regularly reported to senior management and the Risk Committee together with mitigation actions, are 
outlined below.
Climate Change and Environmental Risks:
Ford Otosan assesses climate change and biodiversity-related risks within the framework of its long-
term sustainability strategy, aligned with the Paris Agreement and the European Union Green Deal. The 
Company implements action plans to reduce the environmental footprint of its products and operations 
and to mitigate the potential impacts of these risks.
Risks and opportunities are identified within the Corporate Risk Management framework, in line with 
ISO 14001 Environmental Management System requirements and applicable sustainability regulations. 
These are integrated into business processes and managed in alignment with company strategy and 
targets. Accordingly, initiatives that support the 2050 Net Zero target, including energy efficiency 
improvements, investments in renewable energy, wastewater treatment and reuse to reduce water 
consumption and water footprint, and circular economy practices aimed at preventing waste at source 
and increasing recycling rates, are prioritized.
Climate-related and environmental risks, along with associated opportunities and action plans, are 
regularly reviewed and incorporated into the Corporate Risk Management system. Key developments are 
reported to senior management and the Risk Committee. Detailed information on these risks, mitigation 
actions, and related opportunities is provided in the 2025 Sustainability Report.
Supply Chain Risks:
We adopt a holistic risk management approach that encompasses financial, operational, sustainability, 
and geopolitical risks in response to increasing uncertainties in global supply chains in recent years. In 
2025, global geopolitical developments, the impact of regional conflicts on international trade routes, 
risks at critical transit points—particularly the Strait of Hormuz—vulnerabilities related to the supply 
of rare earth elements, and developments concerning strategic semiconductor manufacturers such as 
Nexperia were among the key factors shaping the supply chain risk profile.
In addition, domestic economic volatilities and growing currency pressure have heightened financial 
risks for our domestic manufacturing suppliers. Similarly, challenges related to financial resilience have 
been observed among certain suppliers, particularly in Europe, due to global economic conditions and 
regional uncertainties. Furthermore, potential operational disruptions at the supplier level (including 
fires, natural disasters, and power outages) are closely monitored to ensure supply continuity.
Our supplier risk management model is based on a multi-layered assessment structure that covers 
financial soundness, operational continuity, capacity adequacy, quality performance, sustainability, and 
geopolitical risk dimensions. In this context, digital risk monitoring and early warning systems used 
globally by Ford are integrated with local early warning and risk assessment tools developed by Ford 
Otosan.
Where risks are identified, their potential impact is mitigated through measures such as introducing 
alternative sourcing options, reviewing capacity and production plans, strengthening supplier 
communication, and conducting on-site assessments where necessary. Actions are carried out 
proactively through an agile approach, supported by interdisciplinary teams established for this 
purpose, ensuring effective risk management. Supplier risks and related mitigation actions are regularly 
reported to the Risk Committee.
Through this holistic approach, we aim to ensure supply chain resilience and business continuity against 
both global strategic risks and supplier-related operational and financial risks.

CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
Information Security and Cybersecurity Risks:
Ford Otosan takes comprehensive measures across its information technologies and business processes 
to prevent reputational damage and potential impacts on brand value arising from information security 
and cybersecurity risks, while also conducting regular awareness and training activities. The Digital 
Products and Services Leadership (DPS) continues to maintain the highest standards of information 
security and cybersecurity in line with the requirements set out in the Ford Otosan Information Security 
Policy, ensuring full compliance with applicable regulations as well as the policies of Ford Otosan, Ford 
Motor Company, and Koç Holding.
Protecting the information technology infrastructure that supports business operations against 
internal and external threats, and ensuring the continuity of services critical to business continuity, are 
among the company’s material topics. In line with ISO 27001 Information Security Management System 
requirements, information technology process risks are systematically managed in accordance with the 
company’s risk management methodology. In this context, an Information Security Forum Meeting is 
held annually under the coordination of DPS Leadership, with the participation of senior management 
and representatives from Foreign Trade, Logistics, Human Resources, Spare Parts, Accounting, Finance, 
Warehouse, and Corporate Risk units. These meetings address information security and technology risks 
from a holistic perspective.
Cybersecurity risks are regularly analyzed and reported to Corporate Risk Leadership and the Risk 
Committee, ensuring that critical risks and related actions are monitored through high-level governance 
mechanisms. In addition, cyber risks arising from suppliers and resellers are periodically monitored and 
reported to the Risk Committee.
The security of Operational Technology (OT) infrastructures implemented as part of digitalization efforts 
is evaluated monthly by the OT Cybersecurity Committee, which operates as a dedicated internal 
governance mechanism. OT risks are regularly reviewed with the participation of Network, Cybersecurity, 
Governance, and Field teams, and corresponding action plans are developed and reported to the Risk 
Committee.
To support a robust assessment of cyber risks, cyber incident simulations are conducted with the 
participation of relevant business units and senior management, enabling practical testing of potential 
threat scenarios and their impacts. In addition, cyber risks are covered through annual cyber insurance.
The company’s information security, cybersecurity, and risk management practices are regularly audited 
by the Ford Motor Company General Auditor’s Office, Koç Holding Audit teams, and independent audit 
firms, and compliance with international standards is verified.
A Cyber Incident Response and Management Plan is in place to address potential cyber incidents. This 
plan is designed to ensure the continuity of digital assets, critical information systems, and services, 
while strengthening operational resilience against cyber threats. It enables rapid and coordinated 
response through early detection, recording, and classification of incidents based on impact and 
priority, supported by defined response and notification times in accordance with Service Level 
Agreements (SLAs). Supported by Security Operations Center (SOC), Security Information and Event 
Management (SIEM), and Security Orchestration, Automation, and Response (SOAR) infrastructures, 
response, resolution, and recovery activities are carried out effectively, minimizing potential disruptions 
and safeguarding business continuity, data integrity, system availability, and operational continuity. 
The plan is continuously enhanced through post-incident reporting, root cause analysis, and regular 
review mechanisms. By strengthening preparedness and response capabilities for scenarios such as 
ransomware, phishing, denial-of-service attacks, and cloud security incidents, the organization’s digital 
resilience is continuously reinforced.
Further information on these risks and related mitigation actions, including the cybersecurity of the 
vehicles we produce, is provided in the 2025 Sustainability Report.

CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
At Ford Otosan, Business Continuity Plans (BCPs) have been developed to address four defined 
scenarios, with pre-established strategies and procedures covering risks that may lead to business 
interruption, ranging from natural disasters such as earthquakes, fires, floods, and hail to machinery 
damage. These plans ensure the uninterrupted continuation of critical processes until operations return 
to normal following the restoration of any damage. Business impact analyses are conducted on a 
continuous and regular basis, and BCPs are updated accordingly. Regular training is provided on these 
plans, and their effectiveness is tested annually across all locations through drills.
Within the BCP framework, the IT applications required to ensure business continuity are identified, 
and the DPS reviews and updates the business interruption level requirements of IT applications and 
infrastructure as needed. Based on the BCPs, Disaster Recovery (DR) plans are developed on a scenario 
basis under the guidance of DPS Leadership, and IT system infrastructures and DR plans are updated 
accordingly. In 2025, for certain applications deemed critical to business continuity, cloud platform–
supported hybrid architectures were deployed to further enhance continuity and minimize disruption 
risks.
To verify the effectiveness of Disaster Recovery Plans, a risk-based disaster recovery test planning 
structure has been established. Risks are categorized and scored on an application basis, and Disaster 
Recovery Tests are planned accordingly based on risk scores.
As a result of these efforts, the company’s preparedness has been strengthened in terms of the 
controlled and rapid recovery of systems in the event of disasters or disruptions.
In 2025, in addition to Business Continuity Plans for production processes, Normalization Plans (NPs) 
were developed to effectively manage repair and control processes following a disaster, and these plans 
were integrated into the BCP framework. Training, testing, updating, and drill processes for NPs will be 
implemented company-wide starting in 2026, in parallel with the annual BCP cycle.
Risks related to natural disasters are identified across all campuses, and mitigation activities are 
regularly monitored.
Eight dedicated task teams have been established to enhance earthquake preparedness, and they carry 
out their activities on an ongoing basis. The Ford Otosan Search and Rescue Team (FOKE) conducted 
extensive work throughout 2025 in the areas of earthquake training, drills, and accreditation. In this 
context, the team received training from organizations such as AKUT and AFAD to strengthen its 
expertise and operational capabilities, and participated in various collaborative programs. Internal 
training efforts included earthquake simulation training across all facilities, online earthquake search 
and rescue training for dealership employees, emergency response training for hearing-impaired 
employees, and fire extinguishing training.
We also require our critical manufacturers and distributors to undergo earthquake assessments and 
encourage them to carry out necessary improvement actions.
Ford Otosan protects its assets against damage, hazards, and losses arising from incidents such as 
earthquakes, fires, and floods through Fixed Asset and Business Interruption insurance, within policy 
limits. In addition, losses related to revenue interruption and business disruption resulting from damage 
to domestic and international suppliers and dealers due to events covered under these policies are 
also insured within defined limits. Furthermore, transportation insurance is obtained to manage risks 
affecting supply chain continuity.
At Ford Otosan, the Corporate Risk Management and Corporate Insurance units operate through a 
coordinated and integrated model across risk identification, prevention, and transfer processes. In 
this context, strategic and operational risk analyses are systematically conducted by Corporate Risk 
Management, and the outputs are integrated into Corporate Insurance processes to support the 
effective execution of insurance programs. This approach enables the early identification of risks, their 
effective management, and their transfer through appropriate insurance coverage where necessary, 
thereby establishing a sustainable assurance framework across the company.
In addition to Fixed Asset and Business Interruption insurance, the company maintains coverage for 
various liability risks that may arise from its operations, as well as political violence risks that may affect 
operational processes. Accordingly, identified risks are covered within the scope and limits of relevant 
insurance policies, supporting both regulatory compliance and operational and financial sustainability. 
Cyber insurance is also maintained to cover potential cyber risks.
Legal and Compliance Risks:
The Legal and Compliance Directorate regularly monitors legislative developments and implements 
measures to mitigate legal risks. In 2021, a Compliance Program was adopted and implemented by 
the Board of Directors to ensure compliance with all applicable regulations, with a particular focus on 
personal data protection, competition law, anti-corruption, sanctions, and export controls.
Within the scope of the Compliance Program, relevant policies and procedures are regularly reviewed, 
and awareness, training, and audit activities are conducted. Through due diligence processes, detailed 
risk analyses are carried out for key compliance risk areas, the risk map is updated, and corresponding 
action plans are developed and implemented. Further information on these topics is provided in the 
Sustainability Report.
Material disputes, as well as significant legal developments and risks requiring action, are reported to 
the Independent Board Members, the Audit Committee, and the Risk Committee, and corresponding 
action plans are determined and monitored.

CORPORATE RISK MANAGEMENT
Financial Risks:
Credit Risk:
The Direct Debit System (DDS), used for dealer receivables arising from domestic vehicle and spare parts 
sales, serves as an effective tool for securing receivables. Most of the export sales are made to Ford 
Motor Company and its affiliates with an average term of 14 days. Exports to parties other than Ford 
Motor Company are secured through letters of credit, guarantee letters, export receivable insurance, 
bank limits or advance payments. Domestic sales are carried out under the DDS and secured by bank 
guarantee letters.
Liquidity Risk:
Prudent liquidity risk management involves maintaining sufficient cash and marketable securities, 
ensuring access to adequate credit facilities and funding sources, and retaining the ability to close 
market positions when necessary. The risk of meeting current and future debt obligations is managed 
by maintaining access to a sufficient number of high-quality lenders. To manage liquidity risk, the 
company maintains sufficient cash reserves, credit lines, and factoring capacity to cover 21 days of cash 
outflows. In this context, the Group has credit line agreements totaling Euro 100 million and factoring 
agreements amounting to Euro 120 million with banks and factoring companies, which can be utilized as 
needed. As of December 31, 2025, net loans totaled TL 99,194 million.
Interest Risk:
The company manages interest rate risk by balancing the maturities of interest-sensitive assets 
and liabilities, thereby creating a natural hedge. Interest-bearing assets are invested in short-term 
instruments. When necessary, exposure to interest rate fluctuations arising from variable-rate bank 
loans is mitigated through the limited use of interest rate swap agreements. As a policy, a 50%/50% 
fixed-to-variable interest rate mix is targeted for long-term borrowings.
Foreign Currency Risk: 
The company maintains a policy of keeping its net foreign currency position at approximately 10% of 
equity. To mitigate exchange rate risk arising from a portion of long-term Euro-denominated investment 
loans, protection is secured through export agreements with Ford Motor Company. Investment loans are 
linked to the acquisition of tangible fixed assets, and a portion of the related exchange rate differences 
and interest expenses is capitalized as part of asset costs. As of December 31, 2025, the net foreign 
currency position was positive at TL 9,122 million. In 2025, total exports amounted to TL 490,781 million, 
while imports totaled TL 305,290 million.
Capital Risk: 
In managing its capital, Ford Otosan aims to maximize shareholder value, establish an optimal capital 
structure to reduce the cost of capital, and ensure the continuity of its operations. The company 
monitors its capital structure using the Net Total Financial Debt / Earnings Before Interest, Taxes, 
Depreciation, and Amortization (EBITDA) ratio, with a target of maintaining this ratio below 3.5.

INTERNAL CONTROL SYSTEM AND INTERNAL 
AUDIT
At Ford Otosan, the Internal Control Leadership, which directly reports to the Finance Leadership, 
coordinates the efforts to achieve the targets for operational effectiveness and efficiency, reliable 
financial reporting and compliance with legislation, policies and procedures at adequate levels. In 
addition to following the policies, procedures and guidelines included in process flows as key parts of 
the internal control system, we also apply an annual internal control plan that covers all the company 
operations. Through this system, 1,338 check points are tested annually as an assurance to the Board 
of Directors and shareholders related to the accuracy of the Company’s operations. At the same time, 
a risk-focused, integrated, and sustainable control structure is established by taking into account the 
outcomes of the annual operational risk assessments.
The Audit Committee, under the Board of Directors, periodically monitors all internal control and 
internal audit activities and reviews and approves annual audit plans. The Committee also submits 
recommendations when needed and opinions on the financial reports to the Board of Directors.
Koç Holding and Ford Motor Company, the main shareholders, and the Internal Audit Leadership perform 
regular annual audits within the company. Ford Otosan’s authorized dealers and service centers are also 
audited for their transactions with Ford Otosan periodically by the dealer audit function of the Internal 
Control Leadership. Reports and complaints communicated regarding Ford Otosan’s activities are 
audited by the dealer audit function within the Internal Audit department with the participation of the 
relevant units.
The results of these activities are regularly reported to the Audit Committee, which is solely composed 
of Independent Board Members.
CREDIT RATING:
Our Company’s international credit ratings have been affirmed at BB+ (Stable) by Fitch and BB− (Stable) 
by S&P. Meanwhile, JCR Eurasia Rating has assigned our long-term national rating at AAA (tr), short-term 
national rating at J1+ (tr), and international ratings at BB (Stable).

SUSTAINABILITY REPORTING
Ford Otosan shapes all its business processes around its sustainability strategy to achieve 
sustainable growth in all areas in the long term. As a signatory and member of the United 
Nations Global Compact (UNGC), we manage all our processes in a manner to contribute 
to the Sustainable Development Goals. Our commitment extends to global standards, 
including the UN Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights, the International 
Labor Organization (ILO) Declaration on Fundamental Principles and Rights at Work, and 
the Organization for Economic Co-operation and Development (OECD) Guidelines for 
Multinational Enterprises. We regularly respond to CDP’s climate and water programs. In 
2022, we submitted our emission reduction targets to the Science Based Targets initiative 
(SBTi), who validated our short- and long-term emission reduction targets in 2025.
Ford Otosan conducts its sustainability reporting in accordance with internationally 
accepted standards, and presents information and data on its environmental, social, 
and governance (ESG) performance to its stakeholders in a transparent and comparable 
manner. ESG data from the independent assurance for 2025 is provided in the Ford 
Otosan 2025 Sustainability Report.
The Capital Markets Board (CMB) Sustainability Principles Compliance Framework, 
published in 2020, outlines the fundamental principles that publicly traded companies 
are expected to disclose regarding their environmental, social, and corporate governance 
efforts. Accordingly, all our activities are presented to our stakeholders not only in our 
annual reports but also in more detail in our sustainability reports and on our website.
Ford Otosan closely monitors national and international expectations in the field of 
sustainability reporting when publishing its reports, and regularly updates its content 
in line with regulatory developments and stakeholder needs, ensuring compliance with 
relevant reporting frameworks, primarily GRI Standards, TCFD recommendations, and 
TSRS.
Within this approach, our first TSRS-Compliant Sustainability Report, prepared according 
to TSRS (Turkish Sustainability Reporting Standards) and published in 2025, represents 
a significant step in integrating our company’s climate-related risks and opportunities, 
governance framework, strategic approach, significant risks and opportunities, and related 
metrics and targets in a transparent and holistic manner.
To view sustainability reports and TSRS-compliant sustainability reports, please click here.
Material Topics
At Ford Otosan, we shape our sustainability strategy and roadmap to focus on the most critical environmental, 
social, and governance (ESG) aspects of our business model. To determine our material topics, we conducted a 
double materiality assessment in 2024, incorporating global developments, regulations, international standards, 
and the expectations of all stakeholder groups. The identified topics are reviewed regularly. This assessment 
formed the basis for determining our priorities by considering both the environmental and social impacts of 
our operations (impact materiality) and the potential financial impacts of sustainability issues on our business 
(financial materiality). The materials topics identified as a result of this assessment are used as a reference in 
developing our targets, investment decisions, risk and opportunity management approach, and how we monitor 
our sustainability performance.

Ford Otosan addresses climate-related risks and opportunities as a strategic priority and adopts a holistic management 
approach focused on emission reduction and resilience. Performance in this area was affirmed with measurable 
outcomes, validation of the net-zero targets by SBTi in 2025, sourcing 100% renewable electricity, and low-carbon 
logistics (block train).
Ford Otosan manages its product range by focusing on electrification and reducing emissions along the life cycle to align 
with a low-carbon economy. Launching an electric version of each nameplate in the portfolio and manufacturing an all-
electric Puma at the Craiova plant advanced the transition process in 2025.
Ford Otosan adopts a responsible sourcing approach to reduce environmental, social, and ethical risks across the 
supply chain and aims to increase operational resilience. Expanding the scope of the Supplier Sustainability Committee, 
conducting surveys, hosting a Supplier Summit and presenting awards supported the improvement of supplier 
performance.
Ford Otosan proactively monitors changes in national and international regulations and carries out compliance and 
risk management activities as an integral part of corporate governance processes. In 2025, the first TSRS-Compliant 
Sustainability Report was published, while product compliance for prototype production and testing processes for 
alignment with Euro 7 standards was supported with firm steps.
Ford Otosan views digitalization, connected vehicles, and autonomous solutions as a whole strategic growth area and 
manages its R&D and technology investments accordingly. Launching 52 new projects in 2025, securing funding through 
Horizon Europe projects, and the Yeniköy Plant’s inclusion in the Global Lighthouse Network are among the outcomes of 
this approach.
Ford Otosan prioritizes a safe work environment and employee well-being and focuses on continuously fostering an 
occupational health and safety culture. Emergency response training, specific OHS training including battery fires, and 
digital risk/incident reporting systems strengthened preventive management in 2025.
Ford Otosan addresses talent management as a core component of long-term corporate resilience to support the 
competencies required by technological transformation. Practices such as the implementation of the Employer Brand 
Strategic Roadmap in 2025 and the ongoing mentoring programs in Romania and Türkiye supported employee 
development and engagement.
Ford Otosan manages quality and safety along the product lifecycle with a systematical approach to maintain customer 
trust and contribute to road safety. In 2025, the Euro 7 compliance activities, including prototype production and testing 
processes, demonstrated that compliance with product requirements was managed effectively.
Ford Otosan addresses clean technology and alternative fuel solutions as critical component of competitiveness and 
sustainable mobility transition. In 2025, this technology focus was translated into solid projects, such as hydrogen R&D 
projects, circular economy applications (ReCube, ECOPLAST, E-Solefin), and the FO&US project for giving batteries a 
second life.

Sustainability Management
The Sustainability Committee is responsible for implementing the sustainability strategy and policy, 
and planned and systematic execution of activities to improve our sustainability performance in social, 
environmental, economic and corporate governance dimensions. The Sustainability Committee is chaired 
by the Ford Otosan Leader.
The Sustainability Committee is composed of the Ford Otosan Leaders (General Manager and Deputy 
General Manager), Corporate Communications and Sustainability Leader, Product Development Leader, 
Investor Relations Leader, Supply Chain Leader, Human Resources and Transformation Leader, New 
Projects and Investments Leader, Finance Platform Area Leader (CFO), Legal and Compliance Leader, 
Corporate Risk Management Leader, Public Affairs Coordinator, Ford Trucks Business Area Leader, and 
Commercial Vehicles Leader.
Sustainability Committee’s duties include:
• Defining Ford Otosan’s sustainability strategy, objectives and actions.
• Reviewing the sustainability strategy and roadmap based on the latest changes in global and industry 
trends.
• Monitoring the management of risks with potential negative impact on Ford Otosan’s reputation and 
activities in environmental, social and governance (ESG) areas.
• Providing the necessary strategic guidance, sharing expertise, and ensuring that the best practices to 
achieve the company’s sustainability goals are spread across the organization.
• Following international developments, upcoming regulations and global sustainability trends, and 
offering improvement recommendations to the working groups as needed.
• Offering suggestions regarding the issues raised by working groups and facilitating and approval 
procedures when needed.
The Sustainability Committee meets regularly throughout the year to evaluate the objectives of the 
sustainability teams and to address high- and very high-priority risks and opportunities, regulatory 
developments, planned strategic investments, and ongoing projects. Decisions taken are documented 
by the Sustainability Hub and communicated to all relevant stakeholders.
Operating under the Sustainability Committee, the Sustainability Hub and the Sustainability Area Teams 
support the entire sustainability management system. The Sustainability Hub is responsible for the day-
to-day management of sustainability processes, while the Sustainability Area Team contributes to data 
collection, implementation coordination, and performance monitoring in their fields of expertise. This 
multi-layered structure enables sustainability considerations to be embedded across all processes, and 
performance to be regularly monitored and reported to the Board of Directors.
We have committed to uphold the United Nations Global Compact (UNGC) and the UN Women’s 
Empowerment Principles (UN WEPs) as part of our Sustainability Policies. Our corporate policies and
We have committed to uphold the United Nations Global Compact (UNGC) and the UN Women’s 
Empowerment Principles (UN WEPs) as part of our Sustainability Policies. Our corporate policies and 
guidelines, including Human Rights, Diversity, Information Security, Supply Chain Compliance, Water, 
Anti-Bribery and Corruption, Gifts and Entertainment, Donations and Sponsorship, Code of Conduct and 
Ethical Rules, Environment and Energy, Occupational Health and Safety, Protection and Processing of 
Personal Data, and other matters, are provided on our website.
Our sustainability performance is transparently disclosed on several platforms, and places us in 
leading indices, including BIST Sustainability Index and FTSE4Good Emerging Markets Index. We 
regularly respond to CDP’s Climate Change and Water Security programs and S&P Global’s Corporate 
Sustainability Assessment (CSA). We continuously work to improve our rankings on platforms such as 
MSCI ESG and Sustainalytics. Most recently we were included in The Sustainability Yearbook 2026 based 
on our strong performance in S&P Global’s Corporate Sustainability Assessment (CSA) in line with our 
targets and strategies.
Stakeholder Communications
We focus on creating value for the entire Ford Otosan ecosystem and leading sustainable 
transformation. We believe that maintaining close, transparent, and two-way communication with 
our stakeholders is essential. By actively seeking the views of diverse stakeholder groups, we aim to 
strengthen our relationships, enhance our processes, and deliver greater value. Accordingly, we build 
relationships grounded in mutual trust and integrity, in line with our corporate policies and principles, 
particularly the Ford Otosan Code of Conduct and Ethical Rules.
We collaborate with various stakeholder groups, including investors, employees, customers, suppliers, 
dealers, public institutions, NGOs, academia, and international organizations, in social, environmental, 
and sectoral areas, and carry out joint activities to strengthen the implementation of our sustainability 
strategy. We define appropriate communication and engagement channels for each stakeholder group 
and maintain our communication through regular information and feedback mechanisms.
In 2025, the validation of our net-zero target by SBTi and the publication of our first TSRS-compliant 
Sustainability Report were among the highlights of our stakeholder communications in terms of 
affirming our climate strategy and embedding sustainability risks and opportunities into the financial 
reporting discipline. The same year, we enhanced our supplier sustainability mechanisms to systematize 
our interactions with supply chain stakeholders, while continuing our efforts to increase stakeholder 
engagement through programs focused on employee experience, diversity and inclusion, and 
community investment.

Future. Now for Environment
In addressing the global climate crisis, we position environmental and energy management among our strategic 
priorities. Building a business model that respects planetary boundaries is a cornerstone of our sustainability 
vision, and we manage our environmental impact through a holistic approach under our “Future. Now” strategy. 
In this context, we systematically monitor our environmental impacts through our ISO 14001 Environmental 
Management System and manage our energy performance with a continuous improvement approach based on ISO 
50001 Energy Management System principles.
We manage our environmental performance in line with principles of transparency and accountability and disclose 
it annually through CDP reporting. Following our double materiality assessment in 2024, “Climate Action and 
Resilience,” “Circular Economy and Waste,” “Water and Wastewater,” and “Air Quality” were identified as high-
materiality topics.
TR: Türkiye RO: Romania
* 2025 values are provided in the sustainability report after limited assurance has been obtained.
Environmental Committee
Within our environmental governance structure, activities aimed at monitoring and improving environmental 
performance are carried out through relevant committees and units. This structure ensures that environmental 
risks, regulatory compliance, operational efficiency, and sustainability targets are regularly evaluated.
TR: Türkiye RO: Romania
* 2025 values are provided in the sustainability report after limited assurance has been obtained.
Transition to a Low-Carbon Economy and Climate Risks
We consider the transition to a low-carbon economy a key driver of our long-term 
competitiveness and focus on transforming our operations and value chain in line with 
our net-zero target. We integrate both physical and transition risks of climate change risks 
into our strategic planning and risk management, and develop proactive measures to 
mitigate impacts and strengthen resilience.
Accordingly, we follow a pathway aligned with our emission reduction targets validated 
by the Science Based Targets initiative (SBTi) in 2025. We are committed to achieving 
net-zero greenhouse gas emissions across our entire value chain by 2050. As part of 
our near-term targets, we aim to reduce our Scope 1 and 2 emissions by 77% compared 
to the 2017 baseline by 2034, and our Scope 3 emissions from the use of sold products 
by 58.8% compared to the 2021 baseline. In the long term, we remain committed to 
achieving net-zero greenhouse gas emissions across our entire value chain by 2050.
In 2025, we updated our climate and water scenario analyses, supporting our assessment 
of physical and transition risks. Further information on scenario analysis and climate-
related risks and opportunities is provided in our TSRS report.
Net Zero Roadmap
We structure our carbon transition program around operational emission reduction, 
energy efficiency, renewable energy use, low-carbon logistics, and supply chain 
transformation, and scale our initiatives in line with our net-zero roadmap. We 
source 100% of our electricity from renewable energy across all locations, strengthen 
our emissions performance through energy efficiency projects and digital energy 
management applications, and continue to expand the use of low-carbon alternatives 
such as rail transport in our logistics operations.
We aim to advance toward our net-zero target across the entire value chain by 2050 while 
supporting the mobility transformation with a zero-emission product portfolio.
To accelerate our transition to a low-carbon economy in line with our net-zero roadmap, 
we updated our internal carbon pricing mechanism in 2025 and integrated it into our 
CAPEX purchasing system to guide our investment decisions. To extend the low-carbon 
transition across our value chain, we also initiated a pilot process focused on carbon 
pricing for our suppliers.

Clean Technologies and Sustainable Products
We view clean technologies and sustainable products as a critical lever 
for achieving our climate goals and supporting long-term value creation. 
By introducing electric versions across all nameplates in our portfolio, we 
have taken a significant step toward scaling our zero-emission mobility 
transformation.
In this context, we continue our R&D activities focused on alternative fuel 
technologies, including electricity, hydrogen, and biofuels, in line with our 
targets of selling only zero-emission vehicles by 2035 for passenger vehicles 
and light and medium commercial vehicles, and by 2040 for heavy commercial 
vehicles.
To enhance our competitiveness in the heavy commercial vehicle segment and 
further advance our sustainable product development approach, we signed a 
Joint Development Agreement with Iveco Group in 2025 for new cab design 
and engineering. The new cab design is expected to contribute to carbon 
emission reduction through improved aerodynamics and to comply with the 
EU Direct Vision Standard. As part of our international R&D collaborations, we 
submitted a total of 22 new project applications to the Horizon Europe 
program in 2025. Through the RECUBE project, which supports our circular 
economy approach, we are implementing practices to increase the use of 
recycled materials in our light commercial vehicles. At the same time, we 
are integrating Life Cycle Assessment (LCA) into our product development 
processes to manage the environmental impacts of products and raw materials, 
with completed assessments subject to third-party verification.
Life cycle assessments (LCA) are as a core component of our product 
development and decision-making processes. By 2025, validated LCAs had been 
completed for all electric, plug-in hybrid, and selected internal combustion 
vehicle models, covering a wide range of materials from plastics to critical 
battery raw materials.
We continue working toward compliance with Euro 7 regulations. In 2025, 
prototype production and testing processes were completed for the Tourneo/
Transit Custom and Tourneo/Transit Courier programs, and design improvements 
based on test results were introduced, with undercarriage designs approved. In 
line with our circular economy approach and our objective of increasing the use 
of sustainable materials, we initiated efforts in 2025 to incorporate sustainable 
plastics into more than 30 parts, particularly for our HCV-Truck model.
Energy Consumption and Greenhouse Gas Emissions
In 2025, greenhouse gas emissions from our operations in Türkiye and Romania were calculated using the GHG Protocol 
methodology, consistent with the previous year. Ford Otosan’s Kocaeli and Eskişehir plants are subject to annual MRV 
(Monitoring, Reporting, and Verification) processes under the Regulation on Monitoring Greenhouse Gas Emissions, and 
verified emission reports are submitted to the relevant Ministry.
We systematically manage our energy consumption and greenhouse gas emissions in line with our net-zero roadmap, 
strengthening our emissions performance through energy efficiency measures, renewable energy use, and digital solutions. In 
2025, all electricity used at our facilities in Türkiye and Romania was sourced from 100% renewable energy.
With Mega AI, an AI-powered energy forecasting and monitoring system piloted in the paint shop at our Yeniköy plant, we aim 
to reduce emissions associated with energy consumption.
Until 2024, greenhouse gas emissions from our operations in Türkiye and Romania were calculated in accordance with the 
ISO 14064 Greenhouse Gas Quantification and Verification Standard. As of 2024, emissions are calculated in line with the GHG 
Protocol, and limited assurance has been obtained for the reported data.

Water Stewardship
Amid increasing pressure on water resources, we consider water stewardship a critical 
component of our operational resilience. In line with our target to reduce clean water 
use per vehicle at our facilities by 40% by 2030 compared to the 2019 baseline, we 
are developing water efficiency–focused practices and continuing to reduce water 
consumption and increase water recovery across our processes.
We developed a Water Supply Action Plan to address drought risks and 
strengthened our water management strategy with input from a hydrology-based 
water scenario analysis conducted specifically for Ford Otosan in 2025. At our 
Eskişehir facility, a Well Water Level Remote Monitoring Project has been implemented 
to support the sustainable management of groundwater resources. Measurements 
are monitored digitally via remote level sensors, enabling rapid response in high-
risk situations. We also continue our work on wastewater recovery using advanced 
treatment technologies at our Gölcük and Yeniköy plants, and plan to install a Grey 
Water Recovery System at the Yeniköy Plant as part of new investments.
Through the Wastewater Treatment Plant established in Kocaeli in cooperation with 
İSU (Kocaeli Water and Sewage Administration), we are transforming the source of 
water used in production. By recovering municipal wastewater, we not only reduce 
clean water consumption but also contribute to the long-term sustainability of the 
regional ecosystem. In the first phase, we aim to save 400,000 tons of clean water 
annually, increasing to 900,000 tons once full capacity is reached. As we continue 
progressing toward our target of reducing clean water use per vehicle by 40% by 
2030, we aim to leave a more sustainable legacy for the future.
Waste Management
As a key component of our sustainability strategy, we aim to reduce waste generated 
at the source and maximize our recycling rates by adopting a circular economy model.
All our locations in Türkiye hold Zero Waste Basic Level Certification, while our 
waste management aligns with our Environmental and Energy Policy, ensuring 
full regulatory compliance. The digital waste management system implemented in 
2025 enables us to monitor waste quantities at the source on a departmental basis 
and track reduction targets digitally, making waste data traceable at the facility 
level. Working toward our target of eliminating waste sent to landfills by 2030, the 
Eskişehir Plant has achieved its “Zero Waste to Landfill” target through the conversion 
of household waste into energy via biomethanization. For the Kocaeli Plants, 
improvement efforts are underway as part of an action plan to reduce landfill waste 
by 20% annually.

Biodiversity
We consider biodiversity conservation a key component of our environmental responsibility and aim to assess the 
impacts of our activities on ecosystems while developing practices to minimize potential effects. In this context, we focus 
on preventing and mitigating threats to biodiversity, complying with IUCN guidelines, and integrating biodiversity 
considerations into our decision-making processes. Our approach includes evaluating facility locations, conducting 
environmental risk analyses, and implementing site-specific Biodiversity Action Plans. For preliminary risk assessments, 
we use the SBTN Materiality Tool to evaluate our impacts and their magnitude on biodiversity factors specific to Ford 
Otosan locations, and we conduct global analyses of site-specific species and habitats using IBAT.
Future. Now for People
At Ford Otosan, our Human Resources approach is shaped around the “Future. Now for People” philosophy, centered 
on human rights, diversity, equity, and inclusion, and effective talent management. We aim to support sustainable 
success by fostering a work environment where every employee feels valued, safe, and a sense of belonging, and where 
development is encouraged. We continuously enhance our people-centric systems and design the employee experience 
around health, safety, and continuous development. Our employer brand, Ford Otosan Yolunda (Ford Otosan | On the 
Way), defines the employee experience holistically across five dimensions: innovation, development, career, life, and 
success. In 2025, under this framework, we expanded our engagement with young talent and launched a podcast series 
for the first time, covering topics such as sustainability, future skills, and career opportunities.
In 2025, we began using People Analytics to support HR analytics, enabling more data-driven workforce planning and 
organizational insights.
Employee and Human Rights
We adopt a fair, transparent, equitable, and inclusive experience for our employees, customers, business partners, and 
stakeholders as a core principle. This approach is implemented within the framework of the Ford Otosan Human Rights 
Policy and Code of Conduct and Ethical Rules, and is further developed in alignment with international standards such as 
the UN Global Compact (UNGC), Women’s Empowerment Principles (WEPs), International Labour Organization (ILO), and 
OECD guidelines. Our human rights policy covers key topics including the prevention of discrimination, zero tolerance 
for child labor and forced labor, freedom of association, health and safety, zero tolerance for harassment and violence, 
working hours and wages, and data privacy.
We conduct human rights due diligence every two years, gathering feedback from employees and suppliers through 
surveys and face-to-face interviews. Based on our 2024 findings, we identified priority areas for improvement in 2025, 
including increasing policy awareness, preventing retaliation, strengthening trust in ethical reporting channels, and 
enhancing transparency around investigation and disciplinary workplace harassment and sexual harassment, reaching a 
total of 9,450 employees as of 2025.
As of 2025, Ford Otosan’s total workforce stands at 25,002 employees.

Diversity, Equity and Inclusion
We aim to increase female employment rate in management positions to 30% by 2030 and female 
representation in Technology and Innovation to 30% by 2026. Our target for our dealer network is to 
double the female employment rate in technology and innovation.
Trainings and communications continued in 2025 to strengthen our inclusive culture, with a focus on 
the themes of Inclusive Leadership, Inclusive Work, and Unconscious Bias. In our compensation 
and benefits policies, we uphold the principle of equal pay for equal work, adopting a fair and non-
discriminatory approach across our workforce.
To view Ford Otosan’s Declaration of Equality at Work, please click here.
Please click here to access Equal Pay for Equal Work Independent Audit Report.
At Ford Otosan, Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion (DEI) is not only a goal but also a core component of our 
value creation approach, managed within the framework of our Human Rights Policy and Diversity 
Policy. Our DEI approach focuses on increasing diversity at all levels, fostering a fair and respectful 
work environment, providing equal development opportunities, and building an inclusive culture that 
encourages creativity.
To view Ford Otosan Diversity, Equity and Inclusion Policy, please click here.
Please click here to access Ford Otosan Diversity, Equity and Inclusion Playbook.
We conducted our first Gender Equality Survey in 2021 and repeated it in 2024 to measure progress, 
strengthening our action plan with a focus on gender equality in STEM fields, leadership 
representation, disability inclusion, and neurodiversity. In 2023, we established our DEI governance 
structure and appointed a DEI Leader, enabling a more systematic approach to policy management, 
roadmap development, cultural integration, and leadership target tracking. In 2025, we ranked among 
the top 50 companies globally in the DIAL Global Inclusion Index 2025, which included nearly 100 
companies from Türkiye for the first time.
Believing in the importance of equal representation in management, we continue to implement 
initiatives to increase female employment and leadership representation. After setting a target for 
women’s representation in management positions in Türkiye in 2022, we expanded its scope in 2025 to 
include our Romanian plant, with the goal of reaching 30% across all locations by 2030. During Disability 
Awareness Week, we organized an “Inclusive Working Practices at Ford Otosan” training for our field 
leaders and hosted an awareness webinar in Neurodiversity Week.
As of 2025, we employ 498 colleagues with disabilities across 22 different disability groups, and we 
continue to strengthen our inclusive employment approach by hiring 110 individuals with disabilities 
each year. In June 2025, we participated in a project supported by İŞKUR (Turkish Employment Agency) 
and employed 17 neurodiverse individuals across three locations. With this initiative, we became the 
first automotive company in Türkiye to employ neurodiverse individuals across three locations. With 
the launch of our Employer Value Proposition (EVP) in 2025, we further embedded inclusion into our 
corporate culture.
Talent Management
Ensuring that our colleagues feel content, confident, and empowered in their career journeys and 
enabling them to unlock their potential and take ownership of their development is a key priority for us. 
With our “As a Ford Otosan Employee, I Am the Leader of My Own Business” approach, we frame career 
management not as a one-way progression, but as a flexible and sustainable journey offering diverse 
development paths. In 2025, approximately 10% of our average workforce benefited from rotation 
opportunities. We conducted Career and Talent Interviews covering approximately 17% of our average 
workforce in the same year. As part of our Technical Excellence Journey, we approved 75 new areas of 
expertise and held 32 expert committee meetings throughout 2025. We also treated the integration of 
our Romanian operations into our human resources processes as a strategic priority and updated our 
Strategic Human Resources Planning processes to include Romania.
To foster intergenerational engagement, we continued our Reverse Mentoring Program in 2025 with 
the participation of 94 colleagues, including 47 mentors and 47 mentees. We also updated our learning 
strategy under the motto “Turning Employee Potential into Value by Attracting the Right Talent,” 
integrating digitalization and artificial intelligence themes more prominently into our learning agenda. 
In addition, we launched our Mastering Leadership Program for field leaders across four locations in 
2025, with 14 groups and 237 participants, achieving a 92% completion rate.
We approach employee engagement not only through loyalty scores, but from a multidimensional 
perspective that includes work experience and holistic well-being. We completed our 2025 Employee 
Engagement Survey with an 83% participation rate. In the same year, we tailored our “In-Office” 
meetings to better address the needs of field and office employees, while also enhancing direct 
communication with our colleagues through a monthly live broadcast format titled “HR Special.”
Succession Plans: To support business continuity, succession and contingency plans are in place for 
senior management roles. Mechanisms to ensure leadership continuity—including both planned and 
unforeseen transitions in the CEO role—are defined within the framework of relevant governance 
processes.
OCCUPATIONAL HEALTH AND SAFETY
At Ford Otosan, occupational health and safety (OHS) management is structured to ensure a safe and 
healthy work environment for our employees, as well as for subcontractors and stakeholders operating 
under company supervision at our facilities. Our OHS approach is based on the ISO 45001 Management 
System and focuses on identifying risks and managing them effectively through defined targets and 
programs. In addition to applicable regulations, the Ford Motor Company International Safety Standard 
and the Koç Group OHS Standard serve as key references for risk identification and regular review. The 
Ford Otosan Occupational Health and Safety Policy, established in 2021, is reviewed annually and applies 
to all operations and business partners.
To view Ford Otosan Occupational Health and Safety Policy, please click here.

We set our OHS targets annually based on incident data, report our performance weekly to senior 
management, and monitor it at the Board level. Our governance structure is managed through OHS 
Boards and Ergonomics and Industrial Hygiene Committees, and we follow up on decisions with the 
participation of union and employee representatives. In 2025, as part of the Safe (Defensive) Driving 
Project, we planned training programs across three locations for leaders and designated employee 
groups with company vehicle usage rights to better manage driving-related risks. We use artificial 
intelligence and image processing technologies to monitor safe behaviors and help prevent fatal 
incidents on automation lines. In addition, through the OHS Card application, we support our colleagues 
by placing them in roles best suited to their health conditions and ergonomic requirements. We also 
enhanced OHS management across the value chain by incorporating our OHS, environment, and energy 
compliance expectations into contractor agreements.
As part of our emergency preparedness efforts, we develop response plans for all foreseeable risks, 
including fire, explosion, earthquakes, epidemics, and environmental incidents. These plans are regularly 
updated, and drills, such as general evacuation, fire, natural gas leak, flood, and rescue and evacuation, 
are conducted throughout the year with the participation of all employees.
CUSTOMER EXPERIENCE AND DEALERS
The “Treat Customer as a Family” strategy lies at the heart of customer experience approach. In 2025, 
we continued to apply this strategy as our main framework and focused on improving experience 
management. As part of our Customer Satisfaction Management policy, we commit to providing free 
application processes, 24/7 accessibility through open communication channels, protecting personal data 
and using it only for its intended purpose, and offering effective solutions to requests.
To view Ford Otosan Customer Satisfaction Policy, please click here.
Using our “Voice of the Customer (VoC)” platform, we analyze feedback from all touchpoints, including 
dealers, service centers, call centers, and digital channels, and monitor NPS, overall satisfaction, and 
effort indicators using machine learning-based analytics. In line with our diversity, equity, and inclusion 
approach, we also track NPS results by vehicle type and customer gender, increasing the visibility 
different customer groups’ experiences. We share these indicators with our senior management 
monthly, and we monitor customer retention rates daily to take swift action in critical situations. In 2025, 
based on customer insights, we digitalized pre-delivery checks in the delivery process and initiated 
improvements to spare parts pricing in after-sales processes.
In 2025, we received 6,492 complaints, which were also answered and resolved. On the dealer side, in 
2025, we launched the Dealer Sustainability Newsletter, regularly sharing global, national, and company-
internal developments regarding energy transition and sustainability in the automotive industry with 
dealers. In 2025, we supported the goal of expanding solar power plant installations in 25 dealerships 
by 2026, and continued dealership transformation with energy efficiency transitions, charging stations, 
social standards, and digital showroom applications.

Vehicle Safety and Quality
Vehicle safety and quality have been identified as a “very high materiality” topic in the double 
materiality assessment, and Ford Otosan manages this area as a strategic priority in terms of customer 
safety, product performance, and regulatory compliance. In line with the ECE R100 regulation, the 
necessary type approvals for the electric versions of the Transit/Tourneo Custom and Courier models 
were completed in 2024, and collision safety and accident prevention systems became standard in 
Transit/Tourneo Courier models in 2025. GSR Batch 2 compliant camera/radar systems have been 
implemented in Ford Trucks products. Safety performance is monitored through customer feedback, 
dealer management system data, accident statistics, and testing processes, and design guidelines and 
technical actions are updated accordingly.
Our vehicle quality management processes are carried out within a holistic structure extending from 
product design to production and after-sales stages. In this context, quality controls are implemented 
to include in-station process controls (ISPC), post-production water and road tests, and sampling 
audits, and are continuously monitored in accordance with QOS (Quality Operating System), ISO 9001, 
and Ford Manufacturing System (FMS) requirements. Quality and customer feedback obtained through 
digital quality platforms provide input to design and engineering teams, supporting the continuous 
improvement of vehicle safety and product quality.

Supply Chain Management
We adopt a holistic approach to ensuring supply chain continuity 
based on both quality-cost-delivery criteria and environmental and 
social performance. Building on the Supplier Compliance Policy 
introduced in 2022, we published our Supplier Code of Conduct 
in 2025, detailing requirements for human rights, environment, 
business ethics, and responsible material sourcing. These policies 
clearly address topics such as zero tolerance for child labor and 
forced labor, preventing discrimination, anti-bribery and anti-
corruption, responsible sourcing of conflict minerals, and improving 
environmental performance. In supply chain management, our 
primary targets include achieving carbon neutrality at our Tier-1 
suppliers and across our logistics operations by 2035.
In 2025, we integrated sustainability metrics into our supplier 
selection criteria. Working in collaboration with our main 
shareholder Ford Motor Company, we introduced a third-party 
independent sustainability assessment platform. Using the 
outcomes of the assessments, we identify suppliers with low 
sustainability performance and conduct site audits at selected 
suppliers through independent third-party firms as allowed by law.
We expect our suppliers to comply with the OECD compliance 
guidelines and related sanction decisions in conflict minerals 
management. These are specified in our Conflict Minerals Policy and 
Supplier Code of Conduct. 
The Supplier Sustainability Committee, established in 2024, 
convened regularly every quarter in 2025 to address performance, 
audit findings, and development areas of critical suppliers. In 
addition, we continued to partner with suppliers for green steel 
trials and recycled plastic targets for critical raw materials (steel, 
aluminum, lithium batteries, rubber, plastics).

Ethics and Anti-Bribery & Anti-Corruption
ETHICS
At Ford Otosan, we operate in compliance with applicable laws, 
international conventions to which Türkiye is a party, the United 
Nations Global Compact, and our internal policies. To uphold and 
promote a strong ethics culture, we conduct our operations with 
integrity and in line with the highest ethical standards, supported 
by an open communication and accountability approach. In this 
context, we expect our employees and business partners to raise 
concerns if they witness or suspect any illegal or unethical conduct, 
and we encourage all stakeholders to do the same.
Our ethics management is structured according to our Ethics 
Policy, which was approved by the Board of Directors in 2018 and 
remains in effect. Within this framework, the Ethics Board, Internal 
Audit and Ethics Committee, and Ethics Evaluation Board operate 
alongside an Ethics Coordination Office, which reports to the 
Internal Audit Leader and is responsible for tracking and effectively 
handling ethical violation reports. The foundation of our ethics and 
compliance culture is built on the Ford Otosan Code of Conduct and 
Ethical Rules, as well as the Ford Otosan Ethics and Whistleblowing 
Policy.
Reports received through our reporting channels are evaluated, 
recorded, and reviewed in accordance with the Ethics Review and 
Reporting Procedure. We communicate our ethics framework to 
employees and external stakeholders through email, the company 
portal, internal broadcast screens, training materials, posters, 
and announcements, and ensure that all relevant policies and 
procedures are accessible through our internal digital platforms. We 
also carry out comprehensive training and communication activities 
to raise awareness of ethical principles among both field and office 
employees, as well as our stakeholders.
ANTI-BRIBERY AND ANTO-CORRUPTION
As a signatory to the UN Global Compact, we embrace all the 
principles of UN Global Compact, including the principle of 
“standing against all forms of corruption,” and expect all our 
employees and business partners to uphold these principles. At 
Ford Otosan, we adopt a zero tolerance approach to bribery and 
corruption. As laid out in our policies, we do not tolerate any kind 
of bribery, corruption, influence for corruption, improper favors, 
facilitation payments, giving or receiving inappropriate gifts by our 
business partners. This approach is governed by our Ford Otosan 
Anti-Bribery and Corruption Policy and our Gift and Entertainment 
Policy.
Any bribery and corruption incidents communicated to the Ethics 
Line are reviewed and reported by the Internal Audit Leadership. 
If an act contrary to our policies is detected, we convene the 
Disciplinary Committee and impose sanctions based on the rules 
specified in the Code of Conduct and Ethical Rules, the Personnel 
Regulation, and the Labor Law No. 4857 for office employees and 
field workers. These sanctions include Human Resources Writeups, 
Disciplinary Committee Warnings, pay cuts, and Termination of 
Employment Contracts. The Disciplinary Committee determines 
the appropriate sanction to be imposed depending on the type 
of incident. The Committee also conducts a root cause analysis, 
recommends improvement actions to the relevant units, and 
monitors their implementation.
We also create risk-based audit programs in alignment with 
International Internal Audit standards. The areas with suspected 
system vulnerabilities are included in the audit plan during the 
year. If a possible bribery or abuse, etc. is detected, we identify 
system gaps in the company’s essential functions through the Audit 
Committee and introduce corrective action plans.
We provide internal training to our employees on combating bribery 
and corruption. Mandatory training is delivered both at onboarding 
and every two years thereafter, covering national and international 
regulations, related requirements, and the implementation 
principles set out in our company policies.

Community Investments
At Ford Otosan, we contribute to the sustainable development of 
our country through value creation and support the achievement 
of the United Nations Sustainable Development Goals through 
our community investments. In line with our “Future. Now for 
Community” approach, we prioritize social impact and shape our 
efforts in accordance with our Community Investment Policy, Human 
Rights Policy, Ethical Principles, and sustainability goals.
To view our Community Investment Policy, please click here.
In 2025, we focused our community investments on education 
and equal opportunities, aiming to create impact in areas such as 
equal access to education, women’s participation in the workforce, 
accessible mobility, and climate action, working in collaboration with 
our business partners and stakeholders. We allocated more than TL 
559 million (nominal) to donations, grants, and project activities in 
2025, supporting initiatives across a wide range of areas, primarily 
environment, education, health, and culture.
To further expand our social impact, we aim to increase the 
employee volunteering ratio to 35% by 2030 and to reach 100,000 
women through training, awareness, and financial support 
programs by 2026. Our volunteer participation ratio reached 12% 
in 2025, and to support this growth, we initiated efforts to better 
structure our volunteer activities and revive the “Gönlüm Senle” 
(My Heart is With You) platform. In terms of women-focused social 
impact, we surpassed our target ahead of schedule, reaching a total 
of 115,079 women and girls between 2022 and 2025.
My Dream Future
Through our “My Dream Future” Project, which aims to empower 
women in technology and innovation, we are scaling our impact in 
line with our goal of reaching 100,000 women by the end of 2026. 
Through our education, mentoring, and awareness-raising activities, 
we help women gain competencies in STEM fields and participating 
in the workforce.
Hope City My Dream Future Centers
Through My Dream Future Centers in Adıyaman, Hatay, and 
Kahramanmaraş, we empower communities in the region 
throughout the year by organizing social events, digital literacy 
trainings, and vocational workshops and extending academic 
assistance and psychosocial support.
Women Elevating Women
The Women Elevating Women project, run in partnership with 
Young Guru Academy (YGA) and UpSchool, exceeded its target of 
reaching 1,200 female university students, engaging a total of 1,587 
participants by year-end 2025. During the eight-week program, 
the participants received technical training and also developed 
leadership and communication skills. 20 successful female 
university students participated in a field study conducted in the 
earthquake-affected region.

Grants and Scholarship Programs
To promote equal opportunities in education, we continue our 
scholarship and development programs with a multifaceted

under the Anatolian Scholarship Program, and awarded 
scholarships to 80 students within the scope of the TEV Ford

İyilik Haritası 
Through the İyilik Haritası (Kindness Map) Platform, we connect 
the knowledge and skills of our volunteering colleagues with 
other employees and their families, creating an inclusive learning 
environment focused on health and well-being, personal 
development, culture and arts, and technical development. In 2025, 
over 90 sessions were held with nearly 1900 participants with the 
contributions of our volunteering colleagues within the platform.
Ekim’de Bi’ İyilik Var

Türkiye, we supported children aged 0-6 with developmental 
disabilities and their families. 35 teams and 136 volunteering 
colleagues participated in the project.
Gönlüm Senle
Working toward our goal of increasing the ratio of volunteers 
participating in social activities among all employees to 35% by 
2030, we manage our employee volunteering on the “Gönlüm 
Senle” platform. In 2025, 722 employees participated in 
volunteering activities, including 206 who joined Koç Volunteers 
programs.
